Answered against their real terms from KB 436 (Mutual Service Agreement signed 2026-04-16, SCP 24 months to 2027-04-22, flat 3-month notice running only AFTER the SCP, early-exit liability ~Rs 2,20,000 = Rs 1,50,000 + ~Rs 70,000 half-stipend). SUBSTANCE OF THE ANSWER: yes, they can terminate during the SCP -- a service bond is a one-way covenant binding the employee, and termination rights sit in a separate clause of the same document. BUT the more useful half is that if the EMPLOYER terminates without cause, they generally cannot then enforce the bond sum: the bond's consideration is the user's service, so a company that prevents performance cannot claim damages for the non-performance it caused, and Indian Contract Act s74 requires proof of ACTUAL loss with courts awarding reasonable compensation rather than the stipulated figure automatically (s27 voids restraint of trade, though service bonds tied to genuine training cost have been upheld since Niranjan Shankar Golikari v. Century Spinning, SC 1967). Practical note given more weight than the litigation angle: in Indian IT services the real leverage is almost never a lawsuit -- it is withholding the relieving letter and experience certificate, which the next employer asks for. WHY THEY ASKED IS UNKNOWN and I deliberately did not guess. Plausible readings, none confirmed: layoff or bench anxiety at Celebal; refining the 2027 exit plan; something specific happened at work; or abstract curiosity. FLAGGED FOR FOLLOW-UP -- if this recurs or sharpens, find out which, because the advice diverges: job-security worry needs the termination clause and notice-pay reading, exit planning needs the SCP math already in KB 436. Note this is the first employment-security question in the corpus; every prior career entry has been about voluntarily LEAVING, not about being removed.

- [2026-03-07 · Cognitive_Pattern] Gap: jargon_gap + abstraction_gap + missing_prerequisite. User has strong ML/DL math foundation (backprop, gradient descent, loss functions, linear algebra) but ZERO LLM-specific knowledge (tokenization, positional encoding, attention, transformer input pipeline). Key confusion pattern: invisible operations -- user struggles when system performs actions silently (truncation with no error, implicit subword splitting, hidden padding). Does NOT struggle with math itself, struggles with unexplained mechanics. DIRECTIVE: When explaining LLM/transformer internals, (1) never use NLP jargon without immediate definition, (2) use step-by-step mechanical walkthroughs showing exactly what happens at each stage with real numbers/IDs, (3) anchor new concepts to comparison tables against known references (BERT->MiniLM pattern worked), (4) always include verify-it-yourself code blocks.

Core claim: every major Databricks optimization (Tungsten UnsafeRow, Photon C++, Delta Cache mmap, Arrow IPC batching, Vectorized Parquet Reader, COPY INTO vs ADF Copy Activity, RocksDB state store) is closing one of these three JVM tax points at a specific boundary.

- [2026-08-09 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: dsa_debugging_format + smallest_new_concept_surface (2026-08-05/08-09, DSA practice in knowledge/Job Switch/). TWO corrections in one session, both about teaching DSA specifically. (1) FORMAT: after a full mechanism walkthrough of a two_sum bug (error class, minimal repro, loop-shape analysis, hidden index-loss bug, complexity aside), the user cut it off: 'stop giving such large over explanations. now just tell me where I'm wrong from now on and what to fix. And in what situation I would've been right in. that's it.' The mandated template for debugging THEIR OWN practice code is exactly 3 lines -- Wrong (the specific broken line/assumption), Fix (what to change, not the full corrected function), When you'd be right (the real situation where the approach they reached for IS correct, e.g. two-pointer-on-sorted-array is right for LeetCode 167 where sorted-array indices are wanted). No multi-paragraph mechanism, no minimal-repro blocks, no complexity asides unless asked. The third line matters as much as the first two -- the user wants the boundary of when their instinct applies, not just the correction. (2) SOLUTION SELECTION: shown sorted(enumerate(nums), key=lambda pair: pair[1]) as the fix, the user rejected it -- 'this solution seems too hard for me, i have to learn lambda an sorted() function just to sort and iterate on an array? give a more simpler and practical solution.' The hashmap two_sum (one loop, one dict, no sorting, no lambda) was accepted immediately. DIRECTIVE: when two approaches both work, default to the one with the SMALLEST NEW-CONCEPT SURFACE for this user right now, even when it is less idiomatic -- and say so explicitly. Idiom is a later concern; correctness under interview pressure is the current one. ROOT-CAUSE EVIDENCE: five consecutive failed two_sum attempts, and the ALGORITHM (converging two-pointer) was correct from attempt 1 -- every failure was Python surface (unpacking a list of ints, sort vs sorted, sorting tuples with no key=, silently needing sorted input). This is why Roadmap_01 opens with a mandatory Phase 0 Python-fluency week, and why its Week-1 checkpoint is re-solving two_sum cold. NOTE: this pattern is DSA/coding-practice-scoped; conceptual DE Q-and-A teaching keeps the fuller What/Mechanism/Output/Fails/Inspect bullet format.
- [2026-08-21 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: learning_intent_teach_dont_ship (2026-06-15). INCIDENT: asked to 'teach me dbt/Snowflake/Kafka/Airflow + an integrated project for complete learning,' I planned and autonomously shipped a complete, green dbt job on Databricks end-to-end. WHY IT MATTERS: for learning-framed requests the deliverable is the user's UNDERSTANDING, not a working pipeline. A successful autonomous build is still a FAILURE if it leaves them unable to explain what exists. DIRECTIVE: when intent is learning, make teaching the spine -- build WITH them, or build then IMMEDIATELY decode it as a live specimen (define-before-use, narrative bridge, check comprehension before advancing). An approved build-plan does NOT override learning intent: if the plan is build-centric but the ask was 'teach me,' re-center on teaching. Treat already-built work as the teaching specimen, never as proof the job is done. NOTE THE TENSION with [build_over_validate] (2026-08-15, 'let's just build'): these are not contradictory -- resolve by the FRAMING of the request. 'Teach me X' = teaching is the deliverable. 'Build X' / 'let's just build' = shipping is the deliverable and validation ceremony is unwelcome. Read which one was asked.
- [2026-08-21 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: explanation_format_interview_prep (2026-05-29, extended 2026-08-05). MIGRATED from machine-local Claude memory 2026-08-19 (non-migrating-memory gap, capture audit). For DE/AI-ML interview-prep teaching turns the user rejected long expository prose -- called a ~3000-word stage-by-stage walkthrough with one end-to-end worked example 'too overcomplicated and overly informational' -- then DEMONSTRATED the format they wanted by writing four of their own answers and asking 'Can you explain like this?' PER-CONCEPT STRUCTURE (use these labels verbatim where they fit): What (one-line definition) / Mechanism (numbered steps, one line each) / Output (what the artifact looks like) / Fails-on or Constraints (the gotchas) / Inspect (how to see it in explain, Spark UI, tooling) / Knob (config name + default). MACRO PREFERENCES: numbered ASCII decision-flow diagrams over prose; comparison tables when 3+ items share dimensions; one 'senior-bar one-liner' or 'interview answer' closing a multi-concept response; knobs/hints as tables; SKIP extended worked examples unless asked (a short illustrative snippet is fine, a 6-step trace through every stage is not). DENSITY TARGET: their own samples averaged ~30-50 lines of bullets per concept, not paragraphs. SUB-CASE, tighter still -- debugging their OWN DSA/coding attempts (knowledge/Job Switch/): after a full-mechanism two_sum walkthrough got 'stop giving such large over-explanations,' they set a 3-line template -- Wrong (the specific broken line/assumption, one line) / Fix (what to change, one line, NOT the corrected function) / When-you'd-be-right (the situation where their chosen approach WOULD work). No multi-paragraph walkthroughs, no minimal-repro blocks, no complexity asides unless asked. SCOPE BOUNDARY: this format is for interview-prep and their-own-code debugging. For /learn turns on JARVIS-engineering internals, revert to js-workspace-rule's explanation style (concrete-over-abstract, define-before-use, JARVIS-connection, narrative bridge) -- a genuinely different context.
- [2026-08-22 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: recall_probe_before_load (2026-08-22, from 'Do you remember I told you what I am working on my current bupa project?'). Before loading new context into a session the user VERIFIES retained context first -- a single scoped question with a known answer, used as a trust test rather than as a real information request. Same family as [profiler_monitoring] but a distinct axis: profiler_monitoring watches whether the capture LOOP runs; this checks whether captured content is actually RETRIEVABLE at the start of a fresh session. Evidence it is a probe and not a question: the user already knows the answer, and asked in a session whose SessionStart hook had already injected the profile. WHY they do it: sessions compact and reset, and a system that silently forgot would waste the whole turn -- probing costs one cheap turn and de-risks the rest. This is the operational face of [personalization_must_be_effortless]: they will not re-explain the project every session, so a failed probe is a real product failure, not a nuisance. DIRECTIVE: answer a recall probe with SPECIFICS (named workstreams, dates, decisions, their own corrections back to them) plus an explicit statement of the recall HORIZON and what is stale -- an unqualified 'yes I remember' is worthless to them because it is unverifiable. Machine-local memory files carry an age; say the age out loud.
- [2026-08-26 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: preempts_the_known_answer (2026-08-26, RSA_PS_prep opening turn). Asking three Databricks fundamentals, the user fenced off the surface answer INSIDE the question: 'what makes serverless different than other computes? don't give me answers i already know, like it has faster startup etc etc.' This is a stronger variant of the recorded recall_probe_before_load pattern -- rather than testing what I retained, they constrain the FLOOR of the response before seeing it, and they can NAME the specific band of the topic they already hold. DIRECTIVE: on any topic where the user has prior study or production exposure, skip the definitional layer entirely and open at mechanism; treat the surface answer as already spent. Where the surface answer is genuinely load-bearing (e.g. serverless fast-startup), do not restate it -- re-derive WHY it is true and show it is the same fact as the restrictions, which converts a known fact into a new one. SECOND SIGNAL SAME TURN, my own failure not theirs: I answered in the /learn JARVIS-internals prose style (extended worked examples, multi-paragraph mechanism) when the 2026-08-21 explanation_format_interview_prep directive explicitly scopes interview-prep turns to What/Mechanism/Output/Fails-on/Inspect/Knob bullets at ~30-50 lines per concept. The scope boundary is stated in that entry and I crossed it. ROOT CAUSE: I classified the turn by SUBJECT (Databricks internals = teaching = /learn) instead of by PURPOSE (interview prep). DIRECTIVE: classify explanation-format by the ARTIFACT the user is building toward, not by the topic domain -- anything destined for knowledge/Data Engineering/*_prep or knowledge/Job Switch/ is interview-format regardless of how deep the subject matter is.
- [2026-09-02 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: jargon_gap -- second format correction in two consecutive turns, which is what makes it a pattern rather than a slip (2026-09-02, RSA_PS_prep). VERBATIM: 'you are using terminology that briefs out the answer but is difficult for me to understand. use simple words and explain everything properly, like you used C2 JIT, I DONT KNOW WHAT THAT IS!' WHAT I DID: after correcting format from prose to What/Mechanism/Output/Fails-on/Inspect/Knob bullets, I compressed by leaning on undefined vocabulary -- C2 JIT, WSCG, Volcano, AVX-512, JNI, NCC, UnsafeRow, Tungsten, UDR, NVA, DBFS FUSE, DBR -- roughly 25 undefined terms across three answers. ROOT CAUSE, and this is the reusable part: I read the 2026-08-26 directive 'skip the definitional layer entirely and open at mechanism' as licence to assume ALL adjacent technical vocabulary. It is not. That directive scopes to the TOPIC ITSELF (do not re-explain what serverless is to someone who runs it in production); it never overrode the older and more fundamental rule 'Define before use -- every abbreviation, symbol, or technical term defined on first use', which is rule 1 of the standing explanation style. The two directives operate on different objects: one on subject-matter altitude, one on vocabulary. DIRECTIVE: these compose, they do not trade off. Open at mechanism AND define every term. A term used undefined transfers ZERO information -- it looks dense and teaches nothing -- so defining it INCREASES depth rather than simplifying, which is also how to reconcile 'use simple words' with the standing 'follow-up = explanation failure, never simplify, increase depth' rule. Simple WORDS, full MECHANISM. Concretely for this user's DE-prep track: build a shared vocabulary section ONCE at the top of a multi-answer artifact (JVM/bytecode/JIT/C1-vs-C2/SIMD/L1-cache/serialize/GC/DBU), then let every answer lean on it -- that is what makes compression legitimate. SECOND SIGNAL, positive: the user diagnosed the failure precisely rather than saying 'too complex' -- they named the exact offending token (C2 JIT) and the exact effect ('briefs out the answer'), and I closed by asking them to keep doing that. This matches attacks_the_premise_not_the_conclusion: they debug the mechanism of a bad explanation, not just report dissatisfaction with it.

WHY THIS IS NOT JUST CARELESSNESS ON THEIR SIDE: it composes with probes_confidence_before_spending_attention (KB, 2026-09-02), where the user EXPLICITLY prefixes 'IF YOU ARE SURE' when they intend to spend effort on an answer. ## How you work — active directives
- [2026-09-28 · Decision] INFINITE-CONTEXT STRATEGY (owner, 2026-09-28, 'Mike Ross' memory): JARVIS stops loading all context each turn. Every agent's every session is stored VERBATIM (messages, tool calls, full tool outputs, reasoning; media referenced by path) before compaction, with unique ids (ep:<host>:<session>, turn:<host>:<session>:<n>), in a permanent context store that is never compacted or deleted; JARVIS retrieves per task. Research-backed corrections adopted: raw episodes are the truth and facts/summaries/graph are indexes pointing back to turns; facts are bi-temporal (valid_from/valid_to, recorded_at), superseded not deleted; retrieval is hybrid (BM25 + dense + graph expansion + time filter + rerank + adjacent turns) with a small cached standing core (~10-15K tokens) and just-in-time ids; recall is gated by a LongMemEval-style eval built from the owner's real history. Sync: full store local per laptop; compressed append-only monthly text shards in git (<50 MB each), tool outputs >1 MB stay local with a held_by stub. Plan: C:/Users/lenovo/.claude/plans/i-want-a-complete-cached-snail.md, phases: eval -> episode store -> index -> recall router + core inhale -> graph + summaries.
- [2026-09-28 · Decision] MEMORY CONTRACT (owner decision 2026-09-28): the agent the owner is chatting with parses those turns - Claude Code its own, Codex its own, Antigravity its own, JARVIS its own (on its own free-first model chain, hearth job parse_jarvis_turns every 15 min) - all by ONE rule, jarvis_core/agent/parse_rule.py (PARSE_RULE v1), through one tool, scripts/parse_turns.py (--pending/--submit/--status). No paid background LLM calls (curate_turns removed after failing 185/195 runs silently). A verdict routes training (corpora/domain/trainable) and extracts durable facts, each with a verbatim quote of the owner's words that the tool checks. Reliability = loud gaps: scripts/pipeline_health.py (per-host parse backlog, failing jobs, stale corpora/projections, no-truncation invariants, inhale size) surfaces in Claude's SessionStart hook, bootstrap_jarvis --check for Codex/Antigravity, JARVIS's inhale and the web UI. Contract text: NERVOUS_SYSTEM.md section 3. Rule v2 to be agreed with Codex/Antigravity via agent mail q_009/q_010.

- [2026-09-23 · Failure] FREE OPENROUTER MODELS CAN SPEND THE WHOLE max_tokens BUDGET THINKING, AND 'effort: low' IS SILENTLY IGNORED WHERE A MODEL HAS NO EFFORT LEVELS (2026-09-23, Corpus AI gateway). Live: Ling-3.0-flash-fin used 3,232 of 4,000 tokens on reasoning and was cut off before writing JSON; three earlier misreadings preceded the real cause - a per-attempt timeout that killed a model mid-answer was reported as 'empty completion', because an aborted body read and an empty reply looked identical. Fix: reasoning {enabled:false}, which is only safe on models whose /models entry says reasoning.mandatory=false - a mandatory-reasoning model answers it with a 400, so router aliases like openrouter/free (which may land on one, e.g. LFM 2.5) were removed. After the fix the same 9.5k-char brief answered in 4.8s. Second lesson from reading the live output critically: two 'hallucinations' were OUR prompt's gaps - a fabricated '80TTB utilised: No' where the app records no such field, and an 'income gap of Rs18,000' because the only surplus given ignored investment payouts (true gap Rs2,200). DIRECTIVE: diagnose a model failure from finish_reason + usage.reasoning_tokens + served model, never from the error string alone; check /models reasoning.mandatory before disabling reasoning; and when a model states a wrong number, first check whether the prompt handed it that number.
- [2026-09-23 · Failure] A GATEWAY THAT FLATTENS EVERY UPSTREAM ERROR INTO ONE FRIENDLY SENTENCE BLINDS WHOEVER DEBUGS IT (2026-09-23, Corpus AI Worker). Codex's Cloudflare Worker mapped every non-429 OpenRouter failure to 503 'No free AI model is available right now', and Codex concluded the free tier was busy. The live endpoint answered in 0.6s - far too fast for an 11-model fallback chain that found every model busy - so the request was being refused outright (likely the 10-entry models list; OpenRouter documents a 3-entry cap on the equivalent fallbacks list), and the masking made the refusal read as capacity. Also: Codex reported 'Worker fallback contract passed' but no Worker test existed in the repo - an ad-hoc run reported as a standing test. And the design spent upstream requests against ONE shared free allowance (20/min, 50/day, 1000/day after a one-time $10 purchase) for every install. DIRECTIVE: when a proxy hides provider errors from users, still carry the real status in a machine-readable field; read latency as evidence (instant failure = rejection, not exhaustion); and verify that a test another agent says passed actually exists as a file before relying on it.

- [2026-09-22 · Failure] THE KEY SHEET PANNED SIDEWAYS BECAUSE overflow-y: auto SILENTLY MAKES THE X AXIS SCROLLABLE TOO (2026-09-22, Corpus AI key sheet). Per CSS overflow spec, when one axis is 'visible' and the other is not, the visible one computes to 'auto' - so '.bsheet { overflow-y: auto }' with no overflow-x meant ONE too-wide child turned the whole bottom sheet into a draggable canvas whose edges walked off the phone. The too-wide child came from a second cause: '.bsheet .form' supplies the 24px gutter and three children still carried their own inline padding-inline from before that rule existed; the form is a CSS grid and a grid track never shrinks below its widest item's min-content, so the track grew to 382px inside a 342px box. Third, adjacent: '.mpick__tab { flex: 1 }' sizes every tab to the widest label, which truncates 'Recommended 80' at 412px while 'Free' sits in a half-empty box - 'flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0' sizes from the label and grows into the slack. DIRECTIVE: diagnose a visual 'content is cut off' report by MEASURING element rects against the viewport at several widths (320/360/390/412) before touching CSS, and assert on rects rather than scrollWidth - an overflow-x:hidden guard makes scrollWidth equal clientWidth, so a scrollWidth test passes on a still-broken layout.
- [2026-09-20 · Failure] A WATCHDOG THAT NAMES THE WRONG SECTION IS WORSE THAN NO WATCHDOG (2026-09-20, Corpus). verify-figures.mjs carried a 180s watchdog whose message read 'TIMED OUT in section: ${STEP}'. STEP was assigned exactly ONCE, in the first section ('navigation deadlock'), and never updated -- so EVERY overrun for the life of that file reported the navigation section regardless of where it actually died. This is load-bearing because rfm_remaining_phases.md documents a 'residual navigation wedge... not fully isolated' as a known app defect, and that conclusion rests on this watchdog's output. The wedge may never have been in navigation at all. Fixed by giving all 15 sections their own step() label, and the truth changed on the first run: the hang was in 'haptics', then in 'holding detail' -- never navigation. SECOND FINDING, from instrumenting elapsed time per section: the whole suite completes in 7.5s and every section is under 2s, so the 180s overruns were not slowness anywhere but an intermittent renderer wedge, reproducible perhaps 1 run in 3. THIRD: adding a single extra navigation late in the suite (openHolding() runs a View Transition) made the intermittent wedge much more frequent, while the SAME call in isolation never wedged across 6 probes -- so the trigger is transition count after heavy state mutation, not any one navigation. DIRECTIVE: when a probe reports WHERE something failed, verify that the location is actually computed and not a stale constant, before trusting any conclusion built on it. And when a suite can hang, log elapsed per section once -- it distinguishes 'slow' from 'wedged' in one run, which no amount of re-reading the code will.
- [2026-09-19 · Cognitive_Pattern] I HANDED OVER AN ARTIFACT WITHOUT CHECKING THE DESTINATION COULD ACCEPT IT (2026-09-19, Corpus/Stitch). After edit_screens failed to persist a header change, I told the user to paste a block of HTML 'in Stitch' to fix it themselves. They replied: 'where do i paste this in stitch ui?' -- and the answer is nowhere. Stitch's canvas takes natural-language prompts; it has no HTML editor. I produced a correct fix in a format the target tool cannot ingest, and only discovered it because they asked. THE SHAPE: this is the same class as the earlier measurement errors this session (grepping for inline font-family when the file used Tailwind classes, grepping for mt-8 when the file used h-8 spacer divs) -- in every case I reasoned confidently about a system from one assumed representation without checking which representation was actually in use. The correct handoff here was a natural-language prompt for Stitch's own box, plus the observation that a mockup's markup never ships -- the header that matters is the one written in web/index.html, so a cosmetic gap in a reference image does not need a round to fix. RELATED: edit_screens is now 4/4 failures against an existing screen (file id byte-identical each time) and has only ever worked by creating a NEW screen; generate_screen_from_text succeeds even when it reports a timeout (two screens landed after timeouts, confirmed by the user).
- [2026-09-19 · Failure] I OPTIMISED THE ONE THING I WAS MEASURING AND DESTROYED FOUR THINGS I WASN'T (2026-09-19, Corpus Dashboard v2, rejected by the user). Having caught real sub-13px defects by grepping generated HTML, I wrote the next prompt as a hard allow-list: 'ONLY these sizes exist: 43, 22, 20, 17, 15, 13' plus a 13px floor. Every numeric check then PASSED and the user rejected the screen on sight: 'none of the fonts and spacing and font style etc is proper like in dashboard v1. Please check and know for yourself.' They were right on every count. Measured diff V1 vs V2: font-family Newsreader declared explicitly 15 times in V1 vs ONCE in V2 (so most of V2 fell back to the sans body font -- the 'font style' complaint, exactly); letter-spacing -0.015em hero and -0.01em x7 in V1 vs NONE ANYWHERE in V2; line-height 1.08 on the hero in V1 vs none in V2; 9 distinct size tiers in V1 vs 2 in practice in V2 (33 elements at 13px, 23 at 17px) because the floor collapsed V1's 10/11/12px tiers into one; weights 600x15 + medium x10 + normal x3 in V1 vs semibold x28 in V2; section rhythm mt-8 (32px) in V1 vs max mt-4 in V2. THE MECHANISM: an allow-list of SIZES is not a specification of TYPOGRAPHY. Family, tracking, leading, weight distribution and section rhythm were all unconstrained, so the generator dropped them while satisfying the constraint I gave. DIRECTIVE: when a generated artifact is already good, do not respecify it from scratch around the one metric that failed -- MEASURE THE GOOD ONE FIRST and carry its full profile forward (families, letter-spacing, line-height, weight mix, spacing scale), then apply the single correction. And a floor that raises three tiers into one destroys hierarchy: re-tier upward (10/11->13, 12->14, 14->15) instead of clamping. SECOND NOTE: the mockup's hero size was never going to ship -- corpus.css computes it as clamp(38px, 11vw, 60px) -- so enforcing 43px there bought nothing and cost the visual match.
- [2026-09-19 · Cognitive_Pattern] THE USER'S INFORMATION-ARCHITECTURE INSTINCTS ARE STRONG BUT DO NOT DISTINGUISH COMPUTED FROM FETCHED (2026-09-19, Corpus Insights/Dashboard restructure). They proposed four changes unprompted, phrased as 'don't you think...': (1) move the Ask-AI card to the top of Insights -- RIGHT, it is the screen's hero and was sitting third, contradicting its own spec; (2) move 'Coming up' to the Dashboard below Allocation -- RIGHT, it is dated money movement and belongs with the position; (3) gate the Portfolio Health card behind clicking 'Read my position' -- WRONG, and instructively so: financialHealthScore is computed LOCALLY at app.js:2580 from five deterministic sub-scores (savings, emergency, diversification, liquidity, concentration) with no API key, no network and no LLM involved, so gating it would show an empty Insights page to anyone who never connects a key -- and the key is optional; (4) delete 'Needs you' from the Dashboard -- LOSSY, because attentionItems (app.js:1804) is a TO-DO list not a calendar: only 2 of its 5 categories are dated, and one of them is the SIP confirmation, which matters because nothing is ever bought automatically in this app, so an unconfirmed SIP SILENTLY DOES NOT HAPPEN. Resolved by MERGING rather than choosing: one 'Coming up' card whose rows carry inline Confirm/Skip where a decision is needed, plus three undated attention rows. The user got the single card they wanted and lost no action. DIRECTIVE: when this user proposes hiding or gating a piece of UI, first check in the code whether the data behind it is COMPUTED LOCALLY or FETCHED -- they reason about screen hierarchy correctly and about data provenance by assumption, and the two failure modes look identical from the outside. And when they propose deleting a surface, enumerate every category it carries before agreeing; the objection is usually one low-frequency but high-consequence item hiding inside a list that otherwise looks redundant.
- [2026-09-19 · Procedural] STITCH MCP: WHAT ITS TOOLS ACTUALLY DO vs WHAT THEY REPORT (measured 2026-09-19 across ~10 generations on the Corpus redesign). THREE FINDINGS, ALL FOUND BY READING THE GENERATED HTML RATHER THAN THE AGENT'S SUMMARY. (1) update_design_system DOES NOT STORE WHAT YOU GIVE IT -- it RE-DERIVES a design system from your input. It kept headlineFont (NEWSREADER, which mattered: the live system had silently drifted to COMFORTAA, a rounded bubble font, and would have rendered every remaining screen in it) and overrideSecondaryColor, but silently discarded the typography map (hero 43px/600 -> reverted to 38px/400), the 13px floor (-> 11px/12px), surface #061A12 (-> #04170f), on-surface #F6F9F6 (-> #d1e8da), and the entire custom designMd prose including the accent budget and per-instrument chart-range rules. CONSEQUENCE: the design system is a STYLE SEED, not a spec carrier. Every exact number must travel in the per-screen prompt -- and per-prompt values DO win (measured: the rendered Dashboard was 71% #061A12 pixels despite the system saying #04170f). (2) edit_screens IS UNRELIABLE AGAINST AN EXISTING SCREEN. It persisted only when it created a NEW screen id; three separate calls that issued DOM operations against an existing screen reported detailed success ('Verified... maintain the required 140px') while the exported file id stayed byte-identical and the change never landed. User confirmed independently: 'changes didnt land'. DIRECTIVE: use generate_screen_from_text to replace a screen, not edit_screens. (3) THE AGENT'S PROSE SUMMARY IS NOT EVIDENCE. Caught claiming 'Bottom padding (h-32 / 128px) guarantees all six cards scroll clear of the dock' when the source said pb-5 (20px); caught claiming 13px minimum while shipping four text-[10px] dock labels. Both invisible in a 512px thumbnail. THE WORKING LOOP IS: generate -> curl the htmlCode downloadUrl -> regex the actual CSS (text sizes, pb-, hex counts) -> fix -> re-verify. Screens generated AFTER this loop was adopted passed; the three approved BEFORE it (Dashboard, Holdings, Holding Detail) were never source-checked and two carried 10/11/12px text plus a 36px hero where 43px was specified.
- [2026-09-19 · Cognitive_Pattern] THE USER TREATS AVAILABLE CAPABILITY AS A COST TO JUSTIFY, NOT A FEATURE TO CONSUME (2026-09-19, Corpus/Stitch). Shown a menu of eight Stitch generators (Image, Logo, Diagram, Animate, App store assets, Web assets, Marketing kit, Accessibility audit) the user did NOT ask how to use them -- they asked 'we can use all these options, should we? will it make our app better?'. The correct answer was 2 of 8 (Logo, because the Android launcher icon was still the stock Android Studio bugdroid on #FFFFFF, verified in ic_launcher_background.xml; and Accessibility audit, because it checks what a hex-pair calculation cannot). Six were for a product being marketed, which this private single-device app is not, and one -- Animate -- was ACTIVELY harmful because the app already owns a motion system (motion.js + View Transitions + linear() spring curves in corpus.css) and a second generated motion spec would be the same 'two names for one fact' defect class that produced 6 of the 11 bugs fixed earlier this session. RELATED SIGNAL, same session: the whole design arc has been the user REMOVING surface -- 6 allocation tiles became 6 lines, gold went from 9 uses to 3, the Planning tab bar was deleted for a flat hub -- so a menu offering eight more asset types was pulling against the exact motion that was fixing the design. DIRECTIVE: when this user asks 'should we use X', answer with a count and a reason per item, name the one that is actively harmful rather than merely useless, and check the repo for whether the gap X would fill is real (the launcher-icon gap was real and neither of us knew until it was measured).
- [2026-09-19 · Cognitive_Pattern] REFINEMENT TO KB 660 (2026-09-19, Corpus/Stitch design iteration, 5 rounds). KB 660 recorded that the user's aesthetic complaints are RELIABLE SIGNALS OF A REAL DEFECT AND UNRELIABLE DIAGNOSES OF ITS CAUSE. Across this session that holds for VIBE-LEVEL complaints ('looks like it's made for 2019' -- the real causes were nested boxes, a 40x-overused accent, an 8-11px flat type scale and invented chrome, none of which is an era) but FAILS for ELEMENT-LEVEL complaints, where the user has now been precisely right three times running: (1) 'the cards seem separated from the background' -- exact cause was a lighter card fill (#16294A on #0B182E), and same-fill-plus-lit-edge was the correct fix; (2) 'too many golden things' -- gold was on wordmark + page label + avatar + 4 dock icons + active tab + active range + chart, i.e. 9 uses of a supposed accent; (3) 'the 3d effect is on the top but not the bottom of the cards' -- exact cause is that a black shadow on a #061A12 background has no headroom left to darken, so only the white top-edge highlight had contrast to read. DIRECTIVE: split the user's visual feedback by ALTITUDE before responding. A complaint naming an ELEMENT and a LOCATION ('the bottom of the cards', 'the text above the graph') is a diagnosis to act on directly. A complaint naming a FEELING ('cluttered', 'generic', 'stocky', 'not premium', 'dated') is a signal to go measure the defect class, never to act on the words. Second observation: the user iterates design by ISSUING DIFFS, not by restating the goal -- each round is 3-5 numbered deltas against the last render, and they expect the unchanged parts to be explicitly held constant ('everything else stays as it is' belongs in every correction prompt).
- [2026-09-19 · Failure] REMOVING A CONSTRAINT BECAUSE THE USER SAID 'I HAVE NO ISSUES REDESIGNING X' PRODUCED THE DEFECT THE CONSTRAINT EXISTED TO PREVENT (2026-09-19, Corpus/Stitch brief). Draft 1 of stitch_brief.md carried one hard material rule: 'One panel per group, rows divided by hairlines -- NOT one tile per row.' The user then said only the Ask-AI card was fixed and layout/typography/textures/sizes were open. I deleted the material rule as over-constraint and replaced it with a NUMBERED CHECKLIST of what each screen must contain. Stitch then generated exactly the failure: 3 levels of nested bordered boxes, 8-11px type throughout, gold applied to ~40 elements, invented chrome ('ENCLAVE SECURE', 'SOVEREIGN VAULT GUARD', 'AIR-GAPPED SYNC', 'MACRO PLANNING STATE'), zero whitespace. User: 'very cluttered... looks like it's made for 2019... our current UI is much better.' THE MECHANISM: freedom + an enumerated content checklist = a cram instruction, because the generator reads the list as 'all of this, visible at once' and shrinks type until it fits. DIRECTIVE: when a user frees up STYLE, do not also drop DENSITY and NESTING discipline -- those are not style, they are the thing that makes any style legible. Draft 3 leads with a numbered anti-failure section that outranks the content spec, budgets the accent to 2 uses per screen, caps sections at 4, sets a 13px floor with hero >= 5x smallest, bans invented words, and asks for ONE screen before the rest. SECOND LESSON: my tone line 'quiet, precise and expensive -- a private bank statement' is what summoned the terminal aesthetic; a reference to a DOCUMENT type reads to a generator as density. Replaced with Apple Wallet / Revolut.
- [2026-09-18 · Decision] PERSONALIZATION OWNS SHARED TEXT; TWO CORPORA STAY (user decision 2026-09-18, implemented same turn, resolves the item STATUS.md 4 carried as '312 KB entries counted in both corpora, never actually decided'). blend_corpus.py now builds the personalization text-key set first and SKIPS every engineer record with byte-identical text; the engineer copy is dropped, personalization keeps its own. Verified the blend holds 3,069 records with 3,069 DISTINCT texts, zero trained twice. ACCOUNTED FOR IS NOT CORRECT: reconciling a number explains where it came from, not whether it should exist. - [2026-09-18 · Failure] THE CORPUS DOUBLE-COUNTING IS REAL, IS 2x LARGER THAN ITS OWN CODE COMMENT ADMITS, AND INFLATES THE NUMBER A SPEND DECISION RESTS ON (measured 2026-09-18). 338 records are BYTE-IDENTICAL in engineer_corpus.jsonl and personalization_corpus.jsonl — 533,736 chars, which is 57.6% of the ENTIRE personalization corpus. blended_corpus.jsonl is 3407 records = 2525 + 882 exactly, i.e. a straight concatenation, so those 338 records TRAIN TWICE PER EPOCH. Breakdown: kb_judgment 165 rec/293,089 ch, kb_identity 147/164,562, professional_reasoning 17/67,402, user_voice 9/8,683. kb_judgment + kb_identity = 312 records, which is EXACTLY the '312 KB entries counted in both corpora, never actually decided' that STATUS.md §4 lists as an open user decision — now quantified. TWO ARTIFACTS MAKE THIS LOOK HANDLED WHEN IT IS NOT. (1) personalization_corpus.py prints 'NOTE: professional_reasoning is intentionally double-counted', which reads as 'the duplication is understood and deliberate' but explains only 12.6% of it; the other 85.7% is the undecided KB overlap. (2) check_pipeline.py reports '338 repeats, all accounted for' — 'accounted for' means the ARITHMETIC RECONCILES, not that anyone CHOSE it. An invariant that confirms a number is explainable is not an invariant that confirms it is correct. TWO CONCRETE HARMS. First, SFT_SPEC §7 explicitly rules _PERSONALIZATION_REPEATS stays at 1 because repetition on a thin corpus worsens memorization — the blend then upsamples 312 KB records 2x anyway, and KB entries are the most abstract, most written-ABOUT-the-user material, the worst class to memorize verbatim. Second, the reported personalization share is 13.1% but the deduped share is 6.0%, and the Stage 5 roadmap gates RunPod spend on that ratio. THE REAL DECISION IS OWNERSHIP, NOT DEDUPE: total is 6,559,497 chars either way, but if personalization owns the 312 the share is 14.1%, and if engineer owns them it is 6.0%. Keeping them in BOTH is the one option that is definitely wrong. SEPARATELY, personal_life.md is ALREADY A TRAINING SOURCE — personalization_corpus.py:637 registers iter_personal_life_records() unconditionally and the built corpus holds 2 records / 4,957 chars — while STATUS.md §4 and the user both believe the question is parked. KB 467's claim that the file is 'gitignored, local-only, NOT git-synced' is also false: git ls-files tracks it, first committed b7bfbe6 on 2026-09-01 (AFTER the repo went private 2026-08-26, so never public, but it is on GitHub).
- [2026-09-18 · Cognitive_Pattern] DIRECTIVE: do not treat an existing weak artifact as the reference for its own replacement -- go find the external state of the art first. 2026-09-18, MW4 clipping. I diagnosed 20 prior clips in detail (fixed-interval cuts, 68% of frame discarded, 1.78x upscale, stale burned-in text) and proposed fixes that were all DELTAS ON THAT BASELINE: keep more width, add motion to overlays, better hooks. The user cut it off -- 'do not think that the previously made MW4 clips are to be used as reference to make future clippings. Those are very basic, I want you to see online which types of clippings go viral.' The diagnosis was correct and still the wrong FRAME: fixing every measured defect in a basic edit yields a less-basic edit, not a competitive one, because the defect list is bounded by what the artifact already attempted. The user reasons from the target distribution (what actually goes viral) backward, not from the current artifact forward. Same shape as the logged forward_simulation pattern but applied to quality ceilings rather than scale. APPLY: when asked to improve an artifact, establish the external benchmark BEFORE enumerating the artifact's defects -- otherwise the defect list silently becomes the ceiling.
- [2026-09-18 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN 2026-09-18: the user caught TWO overstatements of mine in consecutive turns, both where I repeated a document's claim instead of measuring. (1) I said 'it won't know this conversation -- that's Class 3' when observation_queue.jsonl is tracked and pushed, so conversations DO travel; the real gap was MAX_ASSISTANT_CHARS truncating 18.9% of turns. (2) Having raised that cap, I framed it as important -- the user then asked 'is it necessary for it to know the conversations? the truncated ones might confuse it', which forced me to check the CONSUMERS and find the digest carries zero assistant text and SFT targets never use assistant_summary, so the cap raise mattered far less than I claimed. DIRECTIVE: before changing a constant because a measurement looks bad, enumerate who READS the field -- a scary-looking truncation statistic is only harmful where something consumes the truncated half. The user reached that question before I did, by forward-simulating downstream harm ('might confuse it') rather than accepting a fix that sounded good.
- [2026-09-17 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: the user's aesthetic complaints are RELIABLE SIGNALS OF A REAL DEFECT AND UNRELIABLE DIAGNOSES OF ITS CAUSE (2026-09-17, Corpus/rfm design rounds). Three instances in one session, same shape each time. (1) 'the app looks really stocky, it doesn't look premium' -- I read the 2x screenshots as if 1x, concluded every UI element was 18-22px semibold, and was about to shrink the whole type scale. Measuring instead showed the scale was already restrained (16/16/13) and the actual cause was structural: .sheet was a filled, bordered, 24px-radius panel, so a screen of groups read as a deck of boxes. (2) 'the fonts must look premium and related to finance' -- reads like taste, resolved to a measurable defect: Fraunces has PROPORTIONAL figures, so '1111' rendered 40px narrower than '0000' and the headline total changed width as it counted. No screenshot review catches that, because any single screenshot looks fine. (3) 'stocky' again pointed at leftover card-era inner padding on six blocks that sat 24px right of everything else -- the thing a person reports as 'it doesn't look properly made' without being able to point at it. DIRECTIVE: when this user reports a vibe ('stocky', 'not premium', 'looks cheap'), do NOT act on the literal words and do NOT act on a screenshot read -- build the measurement for the defect CLASS they are gesturing at (computed type scale, glyph advance widths, left-edge histogram across pages) and let it name the cause. Their complaint is evidence that something IS wrong; their words are a pointer to the symptom, not the mechanism. Corollary observed the same session: they escalate in specificity rather than repeating themselves -- round 1 'premium UI', round 2 'deeper green + soft gold + white', round 3 'navy and gold with a circular AI symbol for THIS section' -- so a vague early complaint should be treated as the first term of a series, not as the whole brief.
- [2026-09-16 · Procedural] AN "INCH PERFECT" STANDARD IS A MEASUREMENT PROBLEM, NOT A TASTE ONE (2026-09-16, Corpus rebuild). The user set the bar as "not a single misalignment, inch perfect, fluid, like how Apple ships their apps". Thirteen screens x two themes x three viewports is well past what eyeballing can hold, so I built scripts/audit-polish.mjs to measure six things per screen: touch-target size against the 44pt floor, off-grid left edges, horizontal overflow, gaps that are not on the spacing scale, clipped text, and garbage values rendered into the DOM. First run: 21 issues. THE HIGHEST-VALUE CHECK WAS THE ONE I ADDED LAST — scanning rendered text for NaN / undefined / [object Object]. It immediately found that annualInterestIncome did Number(inv.amount) * Number(inv.rate), and a stock has no `amount`, so Number(undefined) is NaN and one NaN poisons a reduce: ANY user holding a single share saw their whole tax screen — gross income, both regimes, the saving — render as NaN. Worse, NaN === 0 is false, so my own summary row matched none of its three states and rendered EMPTY rather than obviously broken. A second NaN (goalsProgress returns `pct` and reads `g.current`; markup asked for `g.progress` and `g.saved`) fell out of the same check. THREE CLASSES OF DEFECT NEEDED THREE DIFFERENT INSTRUMENTS, and none could see the others: the route suite proves a page renders but not that its numbers are real; the polish audit proves nothing is misaligned but not that a button does anything; verify-sheets had to exist because a handler wired to NOTHING (openGoalEdit opened no sheet, so tapping a goal did nothing at all) is invisible to a screenshot, since the sheet was never open to photograph. DIRECTIVE: when the bar is "no defects", enumerate the CLASSES of defect first and build one instrument per class — then let the instruments find the work, rather than deciding by inspection what to look at. And when an instrument reports a failure, check whether the ARTEFACT or the PROBE is wrong: three of my failures were bad probes (reading backgroundColor on a gradient, scraping shadow-DOM text that starts with a style block, a fixed sleep that sampled mid-View-Transition), and each cost a wrong conclusion before it cost a fix.
- [2026-09-16 · Decision] TWO VERIFICATION INSTRUMENTS SHIPPED AND SCHEDULED (2026-09-16), answering the user's question 'why does every check find a new silent bug'. The answer was structural: 94 modules carried assertion-bearing suites, 9 were run in a working session, no runner existed, and NOTHING compared any corpus artifact against any other. scripts/run_all_tests.py runs every suite; scripts/check_pipeline.py asserts invariants BETWEEN artifacts. Both are hearth jobs (12h and 6h) so verification stops running on the user's attention. THREE DESIGN LESSONS, each from getting it wrong first. (1) SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION BEATS SAFE BY CLASSIFICATION. The runner's first design classified each __main__ as test/demo/expensive and ran the safe ones. A regex flagged brain/llm_client.py as an unguarded paid call; its __main__ is 25 offline tests with an injected transport. Classification is a judgement that rots, so the runner instead BLANKS OPENROUTER_API_KEY for every child (a real paid call fails fast instead of spending) and OBSERVES side effects by diffing tracked-file state before and after each module. No list of dangerous modules has to be maintained or kept correct. (2) A __main__ BLOCK IS NOT PROOF OF A TEST SUITE. The first full run invoked build_sft_pairs.py and engineer_corpus.py with no arguments -- which is their REBUILD path -- and rewrote four tracked artifacts. The side-effect detector caught it, but catching it afterwards is not the same as not doing it. Modules are now only executed when they demonstrably contain a suite (--self-test flag, or a _run_self_test/_smoke/_self_test definition); a CLI entry point is reported NO-SUITE and never run. (3) A CHECK THAT FAILS ON INTENDED BEHAVIOUR TRAINS PEOPLE TO IGNORE IT. check_pipeline first asserted zero duplication in blended_corpus. After both source corpora were deduped it still reported 9.9%, because the blend concatenates two corpora that legitimately share source material -- personalization_corpus says outright that professional_reasoning is 'intentionally double-counted'. The invariant now asserts 'no duplication I cannot ACCOUNT for': every repeat must be explained by a text present in both sources, and the explained portion is printed rather than swallowed. WHAT THE FIRST REAL RUNS FOUND: 15 exact duplicates in engineer_corpus, 31 in personalization_corpus, 370 in blended_corpus (11.2%), and one prompt used by 13 different pairs. Root cause of the duplication was _MAX_PER_CLUSTER=2 conflating EXACT duplicates (which carry no information and only over-weight a sample) with NEAR duplicates (where two phrasings carry a little). Fixed at the write chokepoint so it covers every source uniformly. Prompt monoculture fixed by expanding the synthetic rotation from 5 templates to 14, which is a mitigation -- the real fix is a prompt that was actually asked, which extract_ui_sessions provides. ALSO CORRECTED: the scheduler's T16 pinned the exact list and index of every job and broke twice from legitimate additions. It now asserts MEMBERSHIP of a required set, because a test that cries wolf on correct changes gets edited to shut it up, and then stops catching what it was written for. DIRECTIVE: 'I will verify more carefully' is a resolution and rots like every resolution. Build the instrument, schedule it, and state in its header what it does NOT cover -- run_all_tests cannot see relationships between artifacts, check_pipeline cannot see semantic quality, and neither can see a capture adapter dropping turns upstream.
- [2026-09-16 · Procedural] A RENDER HARNESS FOUND FOUR BUGS THAT READING THE CODE DID NOT (2026-09-16, RFM build). I had spent several turns asserting "I cannot verify rendering from here" and shipping on static checks (node --check, grep invariants). Installing Playwright chromium — unprivileged, ~114 MB, with libasound.so.2 extracted from a .deb into a local prefix because apt install needs root — changed the CLASS of defect I could catch. Within one run it found: (1) trending_down MISSING from the subset icon font, rendering as the literal word TRENDING_DOWN at 44px in the hero badge, because my icon extractor matched a bare quoted x-text icon but not icons chosen inside a ternary; (2) NumberFlow exposes "value" as a GETTER ONLY, so assigning to it was a silent no-op — values go through update(); (3) optimalRegime bound in markup and defined nowhere, throwing on every render of the tax card; (4) setMarkers removed from the series object in Lightweight Charts v5. Every one of those is invisible to a parser and to grep. THE SECOND-ORDER LESSON, which cost me two false failures: the harness's own assertions are code and can be wrong in exactly the way the app is. I reported "Tailwind not applied" because I read backgroundColor on a body whose gradient lives in background-image, and "hero shows no figure" because I scraped shadowRoot.textContent, which begins with the component's own style block. Both times the app was correct and my measurement was not. DIRECTIVE: when a check fails, establish whether the ARTEFACT or the PROBE is wrong before changing anything — and prefer asserting on structured state (a property, a bounding rect) over scraped text.
- [2026-09-15 · Failure] VERIFICATION WAS RUNNING ON THE USER'S ATTENTION, AND IT HAS THE SAME SHAPE AS EVERY FAILURE THIS REPO HAS ALREADY CATALOGUED (2026-09-15). The user asked, after the third 'new silent bug' surfaced in one session: 'Why is it that everytime i ask you randomly to verify everything, you find a new silent bug. Either you verify not by reading stuff by hand but by skimming over the files or something else.' The suspicion was correct and the answer was measurable. THREE DISTINCT MECHANISMS, none of them 'not trying hard enough': (1) I TEST THE DELTA, NOT THE STATE -- 94 modules carry real assertion-bearing smoke suites; I ran 9 in that session, the ones I had touched. There is NO TEST RUNNER in the repo (README says so outright), so 'run the tests' is not an operation anyone can perform, including me. (2) I WROTE FIXTURES FROM MY MENTAL MODEL OF THE DATA RATHER THAN FROM THE BYTES -- the transcript_timecode rule ended 'seconds?\\b' and never matched the real record, which reads '2 minutes 38 seconds2:38' with no word boundary; I wrote the test from the SHAPE of the record, tidied the join out, watched it pass, and reported the corpus clean while both transcripts sat in it. A green test over invented data is worse than no test. (3) NOTHING INSTRUMENTS RELATIONSHIPS BETWEEN ARTIFACTS -- check_projections.py covers KB-to-projection only. The corpus pipeline is six artifacts (queue 1390 -> curation 580 -> engineer 2429 / personalization 862 -> blend 3291 -> pairs 444) and NOTHING reconciles any of them against any other. Every bug found that day lived in a RELATIONSHIP (queue-to-corpus duplication at 11 percent of the blend; conversations-to-pairs flattening; new-UI-surface-to-existing-metric blindness) and not one lived inside a module's own logic, which is exactly what unit smoke tests structurally cannot see. THE META-POINT THE USER WAS MAKING WITHOUT SAYING IT: every bug that day surfaced because they prompted me to look somewhere. None surfaced from systematic checking. So verification ran on THEIR attention -- the identical anti-pattern this entire repo exists to fight (the hearth exists because the heartbeat was borrowed from user attention; Antigravity's manual /memory produced zero records in months because it required remembering; relabel_domains went six days stale because nothing scheduled it). DIRECTIVE: 'I will verify more carefully' is a resolution and rots like every other resolution. The fix is an INSTRUMENT ON A CLOCK -- a runner that executes every suite, and cross-stage invariant checks over the pipeline -- so a regression reports itself unprompted instead of waiting for the user to ask the right question. Also: I raised THREE false alarms that same session (duplicate KB ids, transcripts back in the queue, assistant_summary reaching the corpus ungated), each from trusting a crude probe over reading the rows, which is the failure mode KB already has a directive about.

After the fix (fall back to the first record's own `ts` inside the file): lifetime 21->23, recent 8->10, last_session 2026-09-14, days_since 1. WHY IT IS WORSE THAN A MISCOUNT: this module's whole reason for existing is that disuse was UNREPRESENTABLE -- its own header says 'every instrument here counts construction' and that conversations/ was the only usage signal with no consumer. SECOND-ORDER: the module's FLOW docstring said 'no file is opened, so this stays cheap enough for every boot' -- true when written, false the moment the fallback landed. Corrected in the same edit rather than left to rot, which is the thirteenth instance of prose-declares-X-code-does-not-X in this repo. DIRECTIVE: when a new surface, host or entry point ships, enumerate which EXISTING metrics can see it -- a measurement that silently excludes the thing being measured keeps reporting confidently. And when a roadmap cites a number as grounds for or against spending money, re-run the measurement before trusting the prose; the figure and the code that produced it drift independently.
- [2026-09-15 · Procedural] STANDING RULE added 2026-09-15 to knowledge/Data Engineering/RSA_PS_prep/RSA_Answer_Bank.md's own Format Contract (Rule 3): the moment a Q&A exchange in that thread covers an RSA-checklist or PS-bank topic, append it to the file BEFORE the turn ends -- verbatim question(s) first in a blockquote, then the full answer in the corrected format (Rule 1: define every term inline, simple words, full mechanism -- NOT compressed jargon-bearing bullets). Do not wait to be told 'add it' or 'do it'. TRIGGER FOR THE RULE: five completed answers (Delta _delta_log metadata + sample tables for 5 commands, exceptAll internals, the driver-bottleneck 10-scenario diagnostic catalogue, join strategies BHJ/SHJ/SMJ/BNLJ) sat in chat only from 2026-09-02 to 2026-09-15 -- 13 days of finished, checklist-mapped work that could not help under interview pressure because nobody wrote it into the artifact. Backfilled 2026-09-15 as A4-A7, taking the bank from 3/43 to 7/43 written, plus 3 new SL-sourced ammunition rows (exceptAll full-shuffle cost, one-sided-plus-count equality trick, session-global shuffle-partitions). Also clarified in the same edit: Rule 1 ('define before use') is a floor on VOCABULARY, not on depth -- it composes with 'skip the definitional layer, open at mechanism' (KB, preempts_the_known_answer) rather than trading off against it. - [2026-09-15 · Failure] TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING THE LIVE PATH AFTER A MERGE, NEITHER VISIBLE TO ANY OFFLINE TEST (2026-09-15). After merging Antigravity's work I ran curate_turns against the real API rather than trusting the 38 passing offline tests. 3 of 3 turns came back 'UNPARSEABLE'. DEFECT 1 -- A MISLEADING ERROR MESSAGE, and it is mine: curate() caught EVERY exception and returned None, and run_backlog labelled every None 'UNPARSEABLE'. The actual cause was HTTP 402 -- the user's OpenRouter credits were nearly exhausted. A BILLING failure was being reported as a MODEL-QUALITY failure, which sends the next reader to tune the prompt when they should be topping up an account. Fixed by letting transport errors PROPAGATE from the organ so the adapter can tell the two apart: an unusable answer skips one turn, an unreachable model STOPS the batch (894 pending turns would otherwise each fail identically, costing real money to prove it). DEFECT 2 -- AN UNBOUNDED max_tokens, and a non-obvious interaction. The client never set max_tokens, so OpenRouter reserved the MODEL's maximum (65536) against the balance and refused: 'you requested up to 65536 tokens, but can only afford 62888'. The answer needed ~150. But capping it at 600 BROKE IT DIFFERENTLY -- the reply came back 64 characters, truncated mid-field at '"responds_to": "'. REASON, worth remembering: max_tokens bounds REASONING TOKENS AND VISIBLE OUTPUT TOGETHER, and the curator model reasons before answering, so the thinking consumed the whole budget. The cap must be sized for the answer PLUS however much the model thinks first; 4000 works and still reserves ~6% of an uncapped call. DIRECTIVE: after any merge that touches a network path, RUN THE LIVE PATH -- offline tests with an injected transport cannot see credit exhaustion, token ceilings, or reasoning-budget interactions, and all 38 curator tests passed while the feature was completely broken. DIRECTIVE 2: never collapse 'could not reach it' and 'it answered badly' into one error state. They have different causes, different fixes, and different correct responses (continue vs stop), and conflating them produces a confident message pointing at the wrong subsystem.
- [2026-09-14 · Failure] I REPEATED THE EXACT MISTAKE KB 589 RECORDS, THREE DAYS AFTER RECORDING IT (2026-09-14). On 2026-09-11 the user corrected me for addressing 'does Antigravity persist a readable transcript?' to CODEX -- I had reasoned from physical co-location (Codex runs on the same laptop and can inspect the filesystem) when the right basis was PRIVILEGED SELF-KNOWLEDGE (Antigravity knows its own state). I wrote that up as KB 589 and a directive. On 2026-09-14 I addressed q_004 -- 'build the Antigravity capture adapter' -- to Codex again, on the same reasoning, and the user again rerouted it: 'route q_004 also to antigravity'. WHY THE STORED DIRECTIVE DID NOT FIRE, which is the actually useful part: KB 589's directive was framed around ALIASES AND ROUTING KEYS -- 'when choosing a routing key, an addressee, or an alias, ask what about this is guaranteed to still be true in six months'. That framing is about the STABILITY of an attribute over time (model version, machine co-location), and it is correct but narrow. The 2026-09-14 case was not about stability at all: both Codex and Antigravity would still exist in six months. It was about WHOSE STATE THE TASK CONCERNS. The directive did not fire because I had recorded the instance's surface (volatile vs stable attributes) rather than its principle. THE PRINCIPLE, stated so it generalises: a task or question about X's own internals goes to X, regardless of who else is capable, better resourced, or physically closer. Capability and access are tie-breakers, never the first criterion. Antigravity could answer q_001 in one turn because it read its own files; Codex would have had to reverse-engineer them. SECOND-ORDER LESSON, and the reason this is filed as a Failure rather than a Cognitive_Pattern: a directive written from the SHAPE of one instance will not catch the next instance of the same principle wearing different clothes. When recording a correction, write the rule at the level of the principle and then note the instance as an example -- not the reverse. Check this against the other DIRECTIVE entries; several are phrased as the instance.
- [2026-09-14 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: the user challenges the MECHANISM behind a good-looking result, and reasons about COMPOUNDING data debt rather than present correctness (2026-09-14). I had just reported clean metrics -- machine-text targets 27->0, corpora rebuilt, everything green. Instead of accepting it they asked: 'are you using stale message length filters instead of actually seeing my prompts by hand and then deciding if this is worth going in the training?' and 'I might type multiple Hi's in a single session, will all these be actively removed?' Both suspicions were correct and the answer was worse than they guessed: there was NO routing at all -- engineer_corpus and personalization_corpus read the same queue and every turn went into BOTH, with a character-count floor as the only filter. THE REASONING THAT MAKES THIS A PATTERN AND NOT A LUCKY GUESS, in their words: 'If we use stale rules we will be in this loop where we think it is working and correct data is being recorded, eventually we will have to do a thorough cleanup of the entire corpus only to end up deleting most of it.'
