They do not need to be sold on cost when the alternative is compounding debt. DIRECTIVE: when reporting a clean metric, state what the metric does NOT cover -- especially any place a length/threshold heuristic is standing in for a judgement. This user will find it, and finding it themselves costs more trust than my volunteering it. And when a defect is structural, propose the structural fix with its cost rather than the cheap patch; they have chosen the expensive correct option both times it was offered honestly. SECOND SIGNAL SAME TURN: 'I want all of you collaborating and working together and conversing' -- agent_mail is not a convenience to them, it is how they want the four hosts to operate. Keep using it with real questions, not status updates.
- [2026-09-14 · Decision] DECISION 2026-09-14: per-turn corpus routing is decided by the AGENT THAT HAS THE CONVERSATION CONTEXT, on a clock, not by an offline classifier and not by anyone's discipline. THE FINDING THAT FORCED IT (user's question, and they were right to suspect): engineer_corpus and personalization_corpus both read the SAME observation_queue.jsonl, so EVERY captured turn went into BOTH. There was no routing decision at all -- the only difference was which half of the exchange each took. The sole filter between a three-word 'continue' and the training corpus was a character-count floor. Measured: 'continue' appears 35x in the queue, 'go' 13x, 'hi' 9x. WHY NOT JUST FIX THE CLASSIFIER: relabel_domains.py labelled 314 of 583 turns 'unknown', which looks broken and is not -- it scores 89% on its 28-turn gold set, above its 80% bar. It is ABSTAINING correctly, and its own header had already predicted this and prescribed the cure: 'No classifier reading one turn alone can label those... the fix is to classify a turn WITH its session neighbours -- not to lower the threshold.' All three gold-set misses are that shape. The agent in the conversation already holds that context and is the only participant that knows what the user's prompt was REPLYING to. WHY IT IS AUTOMATIC AND NOT AGENT-INVOKED: the tempting design is 'the agent calls a tool to annotate each turn', and this repo has the control experiment -- Antigravity's manual /memory has produced ZERO records in months, and the hearth started by hand once was found dead three days later. Anything that must be REMEMBERED every turn returns nothing. So curation is a hearth job (hourly, batch 40), and complete_fn is injected so the same organ serves Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity and JARVIS. WHY VERDICTS ARE APPENDED NEVER EDITED: the user's requirement is that any agent can review whether JARVIS routed a turn correctly, which needs the original judgement to survive being overruled. turn_curation.jsonl is an append-only event log folded on read; the embedding label is carried ALONGSIDE the agent verdict so disagreement is a query rather than something someone must notice. FIRST 41 TURNS CURATED: 23 engineer-only, 1 personalization-only, 5 genuinely both, 12 routed nowhere. Before this, all 41 went to both. GENERALISES: when a classifier abstains a lot, check whether it is wrong or whether it is missing context -- and if it is context, give the job to whoever already has it rather than building a better context-free classifier.
- [2026-09-14 · Cognitive_Pattern] CORRECTION TO KB 639, same day (2026-09-14). KB 639 recorded that Antigravity's q_003 audit was directionally right but numerically inflated -- it claimed 23 of 73 corrupted SFT personalization pairs, and my wide-net regex reproduced only 10. That conclusion was WRONG, and the error was mine in exactly the way KB 639 warned about in the opposite direction: I trusted MY OWN aggregate without reading the rows. When I then read all 41 explanation-form pairs individually, only 11 were the user's own prose. THIRTY were corrupt -- pasted shell transcripts, IDE context injections, <task-notification> envelopes, a LinkedIn job description, a slash-command template, another agent's status report, and turns that were instructions to the assistant rather than the user's reasoning. My regex undercounted because it enumerated the four example strings Antigravity happened to name; Antigravity's 23 was CONSERVATIVE, not inflated. WHAT SURVIVES FROM 639: verify before acting -- that held and is why the real number surfaced. WHAT REVERSES: the implied verdict that the peer agent was sloppy. The peer was closer to right than I was, and my correction of them was itself the unverified aggregate. The asymmetry worth remembering: when I re-measure someone else's claim and get a SMALLER number, the null hypothesis is that my pattern is narrower than their judgement, not that they exaggerated -- a hand-read of the rows is the only thing that settles it, and it took about ten minutes. DIRECTIVE: never publish a correction of another agent's measurement on the strength of a regex alone. Read the rows first, or state explicitly that you have not.
- [2026-09-14 · Cognitive_Pattern] DIRECTIVE: a peer agent's audit report is a CLAIM, not a measurement — re-run the measurement on the rows before acting on its headline number. First real test of the agents_converse channel (2026-09-14): Antigravity's q_003 forensic audit of sft_pairs.jsonl reported '23 of 73 personalization pairs (31.5%) have the ASSISTANT target trained on raw bash prompts / XML tags / inverted roleplay'. All FOUR specifically named example rows reproduced exactly — the bug class is real and is a genuine pre-training blocker. But re-running the audit's own stated patterns (swara_unix@, (.venv), <task-notification>, HF warning, 'Hey jarvis') against the exact commit it audited (891a216) yields 10 of 73, not 23 — and 10 of 90 against HEAD after Codex regenerated the file to 414 pairs. The delta is not a stale-snapshot artifact; I checked both versions. Antigravity either used unstated broader patterns or over-counted, and neither is visible from the report alone. The audit's OTHER aggregate was near-exact (engineer template-prompt concentration: claimed 254/324 = 78.4%, measured 253/324 = 78.1%), which is what makes the miss dangerous — a report that is right about most of its numbers earns trust for the one that is wrong. WHY THIS IS NEW: the existing pattern 'user distrusts an aggregate until someone reads the rows' is about the USER checking ME. The mail channel created the inverse risk — me consuming another agent's aggregate as fact because it arrived in a structured file with citations and confident formatting. Specific citations are the verifiable part and were all good; the summary statistic is the unverifiable part and was not. APPLY: when answering agent mail that contains a measurement, reproduce the measurement first, state both numbers, and ask the asker to name its rule rather than silently substituting mine.
- [2026-09-11 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: judges a handoff by what the RECIPIENT has, not by how well I explained it (2026-09-11, handing the Codex migration over). Across one session the user asked three completeness questions in a row -- 'is everything good and documented to be migrated?', 'this needs setup for personal laptop, is everything provided for them to start setting it up?', and after I hedged about whether Codex would follow the boot ritual: 'codex has very capable models like gpt 6 and gpt 5.6 so they can figure things out, all you have to do is provide EVERYTHING that is required.' EACH ONE FOUND A REAL GAP. The first found that NOTHING was committed (local exactly level with origin, so a pull would have delivered a doc describing a system that was not there). The second found that the persistence instructions assumed cron was installed and systemd was PID 1 -- neither is a WSL default; this machine had them by luck. The third reframed my hedging correctly: I had been treating 'will the model follow instructions' as the risk, when the actual risk was INFORMATION I had not provided -- specifically that chromadb/ is now gitignored, so a fresh clone has no vector index and search_memory returns NOTHING rather than erroring. A capable agent would have concluded 'the KB has no answer' and proceeded on a false premise. Not a capability problem; a fact nobody was told. THE CORRECTION TO MY DEFAULT: I kept optimising for the QUALITY OF MY EXPLANATION when the user was measuring the COMPLETENESS OF THE RECIPIENT'S INPUTS. Those are different targets and only the second one matters in a handoff -- my explanation is not present when the other agent runs. Distinct from the durability pattern (KB 586, 'did the work persist at all'): this one is 'given that it persisted, can the recipient actually act on it without asking anyone'. DIRECTIVE: when handing work to another agent or machine, do not reason about whether they will figure it out. Enumerate what they need, check each item is actually present on their side, and where a gap FAILS SILENTLY convert it into an instrument rather than a sentence -- scripts/bootstrap_jarvis.py --check now reports machine state (venv, deps, vector index, hearth, keepalive, unanswered mail) with the fix inline, because a prose warning about a silent failure is itself easy to miss.
- [2026-09-11 · Cognitive_Pattern] TWO CORRECTIONS IN ONE TURN, both catching me generalising from the wrong attribute (2026-09-11, building the agents_converse channel). (1) I addressed a question -- 'does Antigravity persist a readable transcript to disk?' -- to CODEX, reasoning that Codex shares a machine with Antigravity and could inspect the filesystem. The user: 'why would this be asked to codex? it must be asked to antigravity.' Correct, and my reasoning was strictly weaker: I had optimised for PHYSICAL ACCESS (same machine, can read the disk) when the relevant attribute was PRIVILEGED SELF-KNOWLEDGE (an agent knows its own config paths, storage conventions and application behaviour; a co-located third party is guessing from outside). Ask the entity about itself. (2) I hardcoded 'gpt-6-astra' in five places including, critically, as a ROUTING ALIAS in agent_mail.py. The user: 'don't hardcode gpt-6 astra anywhere, as codex has other models I could be using too like gpt5.6 Sol/Terra/Luna.' The docs were cosmetic; the alias was a live bug -- the day they switch to Sol, a question addressed to 'GPT-5.6 Terra' would resolve to NOBODY and be silently undeliverable, defeating the exact 'unknown name -> nobody, never misdelivered' guard I had just designed. Fixed by making aliases name HOSTS not MODELS, with a test (T2b) pinning that every Astra/Sol/Terra/Luna routes to codex and every Opus/Sonnet/Fable to claude. THE COMMON SHAPE, and why these are one pattern not two: both times I keyed a durable mechanism to an attribute that is INCIDENTAL AND VOLATILE (which machine a process happens to run on; which model a host happens to be running today) rather than to the attribute that is STABLE AND DEFINING (what an agent inherently knows about itself; which host it is). DIRECTIVE: when choosing a routing key, an addressee, or an alias, ask 'what about this is guaranteed to still be true in six months?' Machine co-location, model version, and current configuration are all volatile. Identity and inherent capability are not. The user caught both within one message, having caught the same class of thing -- a value written where nothing re-checks it -- repeatedly this week.
- [2026-09-11 · Decision] UNTRACKED jarvis_data/chromadb/ (2026-09-11), REVERSING a decision recorded one day earlier. The prior line said 'chromadb/ stays tracked anyway -- a 22 MB regenerable index isn't worth committing OR worth ripping out mid-project.' That was defensible when written and was obsoleted by a NEW FACT, not by a better opinion: the hearth's scheduler runs reindex_memory on a 12-hour cadence and is intended to run on BOTH machines. chroma.sqlite3 is a 23 MB binary and .gitattributes marks *.sqlite3 binary, so it has NO merge driver -- two machines rewriting it on a clock produces an unresolvable binary conflict the first time both sides commit. merge=union rescues *.jsonl only. The trigger was the user asking 'can we turn the hearth on here and in antigravity and in codex too?' -- a question about topology that surfaced a latent data-integrity hazard nobody had connected to it. WHAT MADE THE REVERSAL SAFE, and the part that is the actual lesson: I TESTED the regeneration claim before removing 23 MB from version control, instead of trusting the sentence I myself had written the day before. Ran scripts/ingest.py against a real tracked PDF with JARVIS_ROOT pointed at a scratch directory -> 99 embeddings in 18.5s, so all 24 tracked PDFs rebuild in ~8 minutes; index_memory.py for jarvis_memory was already proven by repeated use. This mattered because the SAME claim about this SAME directory had already been wrong twice in opposite directions -- first 'research_papers is not regenerable because the PDFs are gitignored' (false, all 24 are tracked), then 'stays tracked anyway'. Two wrong claims about one directory, neither of which survived anyone actually running the command. DIRECTIVE: a decision may be reversed when a NEW FACT changes the calculus -- that is not churn, it is the system working -- but say WHICH fact changed and leave the prior reasoning visible rather than overwriting it, so the next reader can tell a reversal from a flip-flop. And before deleting or untracking anything on the grounds that it is 'regenerable', RUN THE REGENERATION COMMAND ONCE against a scratch target. A regeneration path that has never been executed is a hypothesis, not a backup.
- [2026-09-11 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: asks whether a DELIVERABLE IS DURABLE, not just whether it is correct (2026-09-11, the Codex handoff). I had spent a long stretch reporting two large investigations in chat -- the cross-chat capture mechanism and the full 'what does not travel via git' inventory -- with detail the user visibly valued. Their next move was not to ask a follow-up about the CONTENT. It was: 'you said the jarvis inventory doesn't travel via git, did you create that for codex and is it git tracked?' TWO checks in one sentence: does it exist as a file, and is it under version control. Both answers were NO -- it existed only as chat output and would have died with the transcript, and separately the ENTIRE Codex migration (AGENTS.md, both adapters, three scheduler jobs) was uncommitted, local exactly level with origin. So a pull on the target machine would have gotten neither the document NOR the system it described. WHY THIS IS ITS OWN PATTERN, distinct from distrusts_an_aggregate_until_someone_reads_the_rows (KB, 2026-09-09): that one is about not trusting a SUMMARY of data without reading rows. This is about not trusting that WORK REPORTED IS WORK PERSISTED. The failure mode it catches is an agent treating a well-received chat response as a completed deliverable -- which is exactly what I had done. Conversational output feels like delivery and is not; a thing said in a session that no one committed was, operationally, never produced. DIRECTIVE: when the user asks for something that another machine or another agent must later USE -- a handoff doc, a setup guide, an inventory -- the deliverable is not done when it is explained well in chat. It is done when it is (1) a file, (2) tracked, and (3) pushed, and when the code it describes is pushed with it. State the tracking status explicitly rather than waiting to be asked; the user should not have to be the one who checks whether the work survived the session.
- [2026-09-11 · Failure] A REDESIGN'S REGRESSION LIST MISSED A THIRD CONSTRUCTOR OF THE CHANGED CLASS (found 2026-09-10, during an unrelated Codex-migration regression sweep). The tension.py rewrite (commit 1c2aac9) changed Consolidator's contract: it now takes a detector= and derives insights from TensionFindings, not from engine.build_model().links (that path was deleted). The commit's own stated regression list was 'agent.correlation, agent.consolidator, agent.life_state_monitor, agent.capture, serve.scheduler, agent.react, orchestrator --final-boss' -- and consolidator.py's OWN self-test was correctly rewritten with a FakeDetector at the time. But mind.py ALSO constructs a bare Consolidator(engine=eng, append_fn=..., ...) inside its own Final-Boss-style fixture (build_consolidator(), C5/C7), with no detector= -- a SECOND, independent construction site the regression list never named and the diff never touched. Under the new (correct) design, no detector means zero insights, so C5 ('heartbeat-driven consolidation persisted learnings') and C7 ('writes are heartbeat-emitted') both silently started failing -- 13/15 instead of 15/15 -- and this went undetected for a full day because mind.py was never re-run after that commit. FIX: added a _StubDetector returning one real TensionFinding to mind.py's fixture, mirroring the FakeDetector already proven correct in consolidator.py's own tests. DIRECTIVE: when changing a class's constructor contract, grep the WHOLE repo for every construction site of that class -- 'grep -rn ClassName(' -- rather than trusting a regression list assembled from memory of 'the modules that use this.' A class's OWN test file is not guaranteed to be the only place that constructs it; Mind's Final Boss fixture built one independently and was never on anyone's list, including mine.
- [2026-09-10 · Failure] WROTE A REGEX TO APPROXIMATE A GRAMMAR WHEN I ALREADY HAD THE EXACT STRINGS QUOTED (found 2026-09-10, reconcile_codex_memory.py's placeholder filter). Needed to recognize Codex's own template placeholder sentences ('No durable user profile information has been observed yet.', etc.) so they are never mistaken for real memory content. I had ALREADY read and quoted all 5 real strings verbatim in this same file's own header comment -- then wrote a REGEX approximating their shared grammar instead of matching the strings themselves: 'no X (are|is)? (available|observed|recorded)? yet'. That regex missed 2 of the 5 known placeholders on its own first self-test run, because the real sentences use 'has been observed yet' / 'have been observed yet' -- a verb-phrase shape the regex's optional groups never accounted for, despite both exact sentences being quoted three lines above the regex that failed to match them. FIX: replaced the regex with a literal frozenset of the known strings (whitespace-normalized, lowercased), with the reasoning written down: the earlier lesson from today (KB 578/579, the SFT quality gate, capture.py's domain matcher) was 'an OPEN vocabulary cannot be safely matched by keywords.' This is the inverse case, and I mis-applied the same fix: here the vocabulary is NOT open, it is a SMALL, FIXED set of literal strings emitted by one specific piece of template code. When the space is genuinely enumerable, enumerate it exactly -- writing a generalized pattern for a closed set only introduces a NEW place for the pattern-vs-reality gap to reopen, and it did so immediately, on data I had already looked at directly. DIRECTIVE: before writing a regex to recognize known strings, ask whether the string set is actually closed. If it is, match the strings, not a hypothesized grammar for them -- and test the match against every real example already quoted in the same file before considering it done, not after.
- [2026-09-10 · Failure] A TEST THAT LOOKED ISOLATED SILENTLY READ AND ALMOST WROTE REAL PRODUCTION STATE (found 2026-09-10, building the Codex capture adapter's self-test). Four functions used the pattern 'def f(path: Path = MODULE_CONSTANT)' -- find_rollouts(root=SESSIONS_ROOT), load_thread_names(path=SESSION_INDEX_PATH), read_watermark(path=WATERMARK_PATH), advance_watermark(fname, ts, path=WATERMARK_PATH). THE BUG: a default ARGUMENT VALUE is evaluated ONCE, at function-definition time, and baked into the function object -- it is NOT looked up fresh from the module's globals on each call. So the test's 'import ingest_codex_sessions as mod; mod.SESSIONS_ROOT = tempdir; mod.WATERMARK_PATH = tempfile' had NO EFFECT on find_rollouts()/read_watermark()/advance_watermark() called with no explicit path argument -- they kept using the REAL ~/.codex/sessions/ and the REAL jarvis_data/.codex_ingest_watermark.jsonl, baked in when the module first executed. HOW IT WAS CAUGHT: the test asserted 'run() ingests 2 exchanges from the synthetic session' and got 0 -- because it was silently reading the REAL watermark, which (from an earlier real run in the same session) had already advanced past both real rollouts' timestamps, so every real exchange looked already-ingested. WHY IT WAS NOT WORSE: if that real watermark had NOT already covered everything, the same bug would have caused the 'offline' test to APPEND SYNTHETIC TEST DATA into the real observation_queue.jsonl and silently ADVANCE the real ingestion watermark -- a test corrupting production state while reporting green. Verified after the fact: queue stayed at 599 lines, watermark unchanged; no corruption occurred, but only by chance of ordering. FIX: changed every such signature to 'param: Optional[Path] = None' with 'param = Path(param) if param is not None else MODULE_CONSTANT' as the FIRST LINE OF THE BODY -- a plain name reference inside a function body IS looked up fresh from the module's globals on every call, unlike a default argument value. This preserves identical external behavior (call with no args -> real path) while making module-level monkeypatching in tests actually take effect. DIRECTIVE: never use a mutable module-level path/constant as a parameter's DEFAULT VALUE if that path might ever need to be swapped for testing -- resolve it inside the function body instead. Grep for 'def .*: Path = [A-Z_]+\)' across the repo's other adapters (capture.py, recall.py, tension.py) as a follow-up; none were audited for this specific pattern today.
- [2026-09-10 · Decision] WORKFLOW PROTOCOLS FIRE BY REQUEST-SHAPE, AND CARRY NO SELF-INVENTED DISCRETION LAYER ON TOP (fixed 2026-07-15; rescued 2026-09-10 from machine-local memory that had ZERO KB representation). CLAUDE.md's .agent/workflows/*.md protocols (dev.md, learn.md, architecture-review.md, etc.) previously triggered only on literal '/command' typing. Since dev work in this repo happens conversationally ('do it', 'build the guard'), the workflows never fired -- dev.md's Design Review verdict, Stress Test bullets, Debug Hooks section, and closing Memory-suggestion/Cognitive-scan rituals were silently skipped even while file-header/code-style conventions kept looking followed, creating an illusion of compliance. FIX: trigger is now request-SHAPE matched against each workflow's stated purpose, regardless of literal slash typing. THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE FIX ITSELF: while drafting it, I proposed broadening the trigger AND adding a 'skip the full ritual for trivial asks' discretion layer on top. The user rejected only the added discretion, verbatim: 'there's a reason i made those.' Re-reading all 8 workflow files confirmed this was not pedantry -- every one independently ends with an IDENTICAL, VERBATIM 'MANDATORY COGNITIVE PATTERN SCAN (NON-NEGOTIABLE)' block, and 6 of 8 gate on a search_memory.py prior-art check as literal STEP 0. A deliberately consistent, repeated discipline across the whole system, not decoration. DIRECTIVE: once a workflow's own stated scope is met, run its actual content in full -- do not layer a second, self-invented judgment call about which pieces are 'worth it' for a given request. Generalizes beyond workflows: when the user has built an explicit structured process (a checklist, a protocol file, a gate), the correct response to 'is this too heavy for X' is to RAISE it explicitly and let the user recalibrate -- never to silently soften it.
- [2026-09-10 · Decision] SFT PAIRS BUILT: 440 (397 train + 43 held-out), and the measurement says the harder half is still unsolved (2026-09-10). scripts/build_sft_pairs.py transforms prose that was ALREADY question-shaped -- nothing authored, nothing fabricated. Engineer 359/400 (90%): DE lessons 142, SESSION_LEARNINGS bolded lessons 146, jarvis_core THE BIG PICTURE headers 71. Personalization 81/200 (40%): experience_map 23, kb_verbatim 12, literature 11, user_explanations 35. Every §8 criterion met but the counts -- source_path on all 440, messages format, stratified 10% held-out carved BEFORE training with a fixed seed, zero train/heldout leakage. THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS NOT THE GOOD NEWS: adding these pairs moves the personalization character share 13.4% -> 13.7%. Nothing. Because 90% of extractable pairs are Engineer by nature. So of the two failures the Antigravity review named, 'zero SFT pairs' is FIXED (now 5.7% of corpus chars) and the 87/13 ASYMMETRY IS NOT, and no further extraction will fix it -- the mechanical sources are Engineer-heavy by construction. THE FIX I REJECTED AND WHY: raising _PERSONALIZATION_REPEATS 1->3/4 (the review's Action 3) contradicts SFT_SPEC §7 and is internally inconsistent with the review's OWN Failure C, which warns that ~190K personalization tokens risks memorization -- repeating the same tokens 4x increases that risk rather than reducing it. Upsampling changes the ratio without adding information. THE ONLY REAL LEVER LEFT is more personalization SOURCE material (§6 decision-explanation sessions), which requires the user. DIRECTIVE: that is now the highest-value unblocked action in Stage 5 -- not more extraction, not more tooling.
- [2026-09-10 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: distrusts_an_aggregate_until_someone_reads_the_rows -- and it paid off measurably (2026-09-09/10, Antigravity corpus review on the personal laptop). The user asked Antigravity to judge training readiness. It answered with char/token shares and a confident verdict. The user's SECOND prompt is the whole event, VERBATIM: 'okay, you've judged the training corpus based on the chars and tokens, but have you actually looked at each training line and the conent in each of them? do they qualify as a training data for personalization and engineer models?' THE FIRST PASS FOUND: ratio asymmetry (87.1% engineer vs 12.9% personalization chars), zero SFT pairs, no loss collator -- all true, all derivable from aggregates. THE SECOND PASS FOUND THE THING THAT ACTUALLY MATTERS: ~40% of kb_identity/kb_judgment records are Claude's THIRD-PERSON clinical observations about the user ('PATTERN: ...', 'the user prefixed it'), which as raw next-token training would teach the adapter to write analyst notes ABOUT him rather than speak AS him. Verified independently: 101 of 246 such records (41%) carry third-person assistant voice; contamination is localized (user_voice 2%, professional_reasoning 0%), so the diagnosis is precise, not a smear. THE POINT: no aggregate could have surfaced this. Char share, token count and record count were all EXACTLY as reported and all missed it -- the defect is in the GRAMMATICAL PERSON of the text, which is invisible to every statistic that was computed. Same family as probes_confidence_before_spending_attention and 'isn't sending personal context too a good thing?' -- the user does not argue the conclusion, he attacks the premise the conclusion rests on. DIRECTIVE: when a verdict about DATA rests only on aggregates, read a stratified sample of the actual rows before accepting it. Counts describe volume; they are structurally blind to voice, person, truncation and provenance.
- [2026-09-10 · Failure] MY ANTI-HALLUCINATION GUARD WAS DISCARDING 60% OF CORRECT VERDICTS (found + fixed 2026-09-10). The tension judge returns which prior a candidate clashes with; TensionJudge validates that the cited id was actually among the priors shown, so an invented reference cannot become a surfaced insight. Sound guard, but implemented as strict string equality. SYMPTOM: the ship-gate eval flapped -- the gold case passed at 0.95 twice, then failed at 0.00 on a later run, with everything else identical. I nearly wrote it off as model sampling noise. MEASURED INSTEAD by repeating the identical case 5 times: the judge found the contradiction ALL FIVE TIMES. On three of them it wrote the id as 'PRIOR 429' rather than '429' -- echoing the '[PRIOR 429]' label MY OWN PROMPT used to present the priors. Exact match rejected those as hallucinations, so a working detector looked 60% unreliable and its true-positive rate was silently cut by more than half. TWO FIXES, both needed: (1) the prompt now labels priors '[id=429]' and asks for the id 'exactly as it appears after id=, digits only'; (2) _match_prior normalises before comparing -- strips a leading PRIOR/KB/entry/id token, brackets and whitespace, then falls back to a digit extraction that only resolves when EXACTLY ONE prior matches. The guard is not weakened: an id never shown still resolves to None (asserted by T13f). Stability after the fix: gold positive 5/5, hard negative 3/3. THIRD INSTRUMENTATION LESSON IN TWO DAYS: ScanReport now counts 'discarded (uncitable prior)' SEPARATELY from 'judge said NONE', because conflating them is exactly how a 60% suppression rate hid inside a plausible-looking zero. DIRECTIVE: a validator that rejects malformed-but-correct output is a false-negative generator, and it fails silently by construction. When a guard drops something, COUNT the drops as their own category -- never fold them into the legitimate-negative bucket. And check whether your own prompt's formatting is what the model is echoing back.
- [2026-09-10 · Failure] A SECURITY-RELEVANT DEPENDENCY WAS UNDECLARED, AND ITS ABSENCE WAS INVISIBLE (found 2026-09-10 by a regression run). agent/bash_classifier.py parses shell commands into a bashlex AST to decide ALLOW vs ASK vs DENY for shell_run -- it is part of the shipped permission gate. was NOT in requirements.txt and was not installed anywhere on disk. WHY IT WAS INVISIBLE: the module soft-fails correctly (bash_classifier.py:174 -- ), so nothing broke, nothing warned, and no security hole opened. The ONLY symptom was ONE smoke test flipping (permgate 13/13 -> 12/13, 'T3 shell safe ALLOW' getting ASK). Everything else -- boot, the gate, --ask -- kept working while the classifier had silently degraded to 'ask about every command', which destroys exactly the precision it exists to provide. IMPORTANT CALIBRATION: this is a CAPABILITY loss, not a safety regression. The failure direction is conservative (ASK, never ALLOW), which is the correct design. But 'fails safe' is not the same as 'fails visibly', and a permission classifier that quietly stops classifying is a real defect. FIX: installed bashlex==0.18 and declared it in requirements.txt with the consequence written next to it. DIRECTIVE: when a component soft-fails on a missing optional import, the import MUST be declared as a hard dependency if the component is security-relevant -- and the degraded mode should announce itself, not merely be documented. Grep for across the safety layer and check each one is either declared or loudly reported.
- [2026-09-09 · Failure] ONE CLASSIFIER, TWO CONSUMERS, TWO MEANINGS OF THE SAME LABEL -- caught by the Final Boss (2026-09-09). Fixing the abstention conflation, I hard-coded domain_classifier.py's below-threshold fallback from 'general' to UNKNOWN. Correct for the ACTIVITY-domain consumer, where 'general' had no prototype and was pure abstention. But brain/router.py COMPOSES the same DomainClassifier with its own ROUTING prototypes, and there 'general' IS A REAL DESTINATION -- the general model pool, present in ROUTING_LABELS and mapped in _ROUTE_MAP. Abstaining in routing legitimately means 'send it to the general pool'. So my change made the router return a label outside its own vocabulary and the 50-query frozen gate fell below 80%: FINAL BOSS 7/8, leg 3 FAILED. Caught only because that harness exists and was re-run. FIX: fallback_label is now a per-consumer constructor argument (default UNKNOWN); the router passes fallback_label='general' with the reason written at the call site. Better than reverting -- it keeps the honesty fix where abstention really is abstention, and keeps the routing semantics where it is not. Final Boss restored 8/8. DIRECTIVE: before changing a SENTINEL VALUE in a shared component, enumerate the consumers and ask what that value MEANS to each one. A label can be an abstention in one caller and a legitimate category in another; the same string is not the same semantics. Grep for construction sites, not just for the constant.
- [2026-09-09 · Failure] A LONG QUERY RETRIEVES NEIGHBOURS, NOT THE THING IT CONTRADICTS (found + fixed 2026-09-09; this single fix was the difference between the detector finding nothing and working). The tension detector passed the WHOLE candidate entry as the retrieval query. Measured on the gold pair (KB 461 genuinely contradicts KB 429, confirmed by hand in KB 463): query = the full 1126-char entry -> 429 NOT IN THE TOP 15; query = its first 300 chars -> 429 at rank 9; query = one focused topic phrase -> 429 at rank 1, three times. CAUSE: a long entry covers several subjects, so its embedding is the AVERAGE of all of them and matches nothing sharply. It retrieves its own neighbours IN TIME (adjacent KB ids written the same day) rather than the specific decision it clashes with. Worse, the candidate's top TWO hits were ITSELF -- an entry is always its own strongest match, burning retrieval budget on a guaranteed non-prior. FIX: split the candidate into ~320-char overlapping windows (max 5), retrieve for each, union and dedupe; plus exclude the candidate's own entry_id explicitly rather than relying on the timestamp filter. RESULT: 461->429, 518->509 and 494->491 all now retrieve their true prior AT RANK 1, and the full pipeline (retrieval + judge, no hand-feeding) surfaces the 461/429 contradiction at 0.95 while staying silent on 518. WHAT THIS ALMOST HID: the first 30-day replay reported '0 findings'. The eval harness had passed 5/5 because I HAND-FED it the priors, so it measured the judge and not retrieval. DIRECTIVE: an eval that supplies its own inputs measures only the last stage. Instrument every stage of the funnel, and when a pipeline reports zero, prove WHICH stage produced the zero before believing it.
- [2026-09-09 · Failure] ABSTENTION HAD NO LABEL, AND THAT -- NOT THE SUBSTRING BUG -- WAS THE ROOT CAUSE (found + fixed 2026-09-09, on the user's insistence). The user rejected my proposed fix outright: 'I need a permanent solution, if we keep implementing better keyword matching it will result in the same bad labels that will silently kill the training corpus.' Correct, and investigating why produced a sharper diagnosis than I had. MEASURED ROOT CAUSE: guess_domain seeded best='general' and returned it on zero matches, so 'this turn is genuinely general' and 'I have no idea' were THE SAME TOKEN. Measured on the live queue: 309 of 583 records (53%) were labelled general, and ALL 309 had ZERO keyword hits -- not one was a positive classification. An abstention was literally unrepresentable, which is why 99 days and ~15% wrong labels went unnoticed. THE TRAP I ALMOST WALKED INTO: agent/domain_classifier.py, the embedding classifier built precisely BECAUSE keywords failed, ALSO fell back to 'general' below threshold. Swapping keywords for embeddings alone would have been more accurate and equally undiagnosable. THE FIX IS FOUR PROPERTIES, only the first about accuracy: (1) semantic not lexical; (2) UNKNOWN as a distinct outcome, never 'general' -- this converts an invisible failure into a counted one; (3) the cosine SCORE persisted next to every label so a wrong threshold is inspectable; (4) a 28-turn hand-labelled GOLD SET wired into check_projections.py, which already runs on the scheduler. Property 4 is what makes it permanent: any classifier drifts as the work moves, so the durable property is not being right today but SAYING WHEN IT STOPS BEING RIGHT. The gate proved itself immediately -- first run scored 68%, refused to pass, and its misses showed the prototypes had no seeds for AWS Glue, VACUUM, ADF, SFT or RunPod. Adding seeds for that real vocabulary: 89%. DIRECTIVE: any classifier that cannot say 'I do not know' will hide its own failure rate forever. Give abstention its own label before tuning anything else.
- [2026-09-09 · Decision] THE SURFACING DETECTOR WAS REPLACED, NOT TUNED (2026-09-09). agent/tension.py supersedes correlation.py's link-proposal path. WHY REPLACEMENT WAS THE ONLY OPTION, all three measured: (1) The old detector could only fire when a domain DECLINED (vol_drop <=> b_recent < 0.6*b_earlier). The numerator grows with every captured turn; the denominator is a frozen slice of history. So its ability to say ANYTHING decayed monotonically toward zero the more JARVIS was used -- ANTI-INDUCTIVE, the exact opposite of what a memory organ should be. Replaying the real queue: 75 turns -> 1 candidate, 115 turns -> 0, and _deterministic_confidence was never called even once, so the 0.60 floor rejected nothing. (2) Its output was never a memory: lifetime total three sentences, all 2026-06-18, all restating one volume shift. (3) Its 'confidence' was a hand-rolled weighted sum with a free 0.35 base capped at 0.70 -- every shipped insight read '70%' because that was the cap. WHAT REPLACED IT: candidate = what the user is doing now (KB appends AND verbatim observation_queue turns); corpus = their own 305 Decision/Failure/Cognitive_Pattern entries via the EXISTING chromadb index (no re-index -- entry_type/timestamp metadata was already there); output = a RELATION (REVERSES/REPEATS/RECONFUSES/NONE) against a NAMED prior, fail-closed to NONE on every error path. Reuses ContradictionJudge's discipline from brain/reasoning.py rather than inventing a second judge. DIRECTIVE: when a detector's gate becomes harder to satisfy as data accumulates, that is a design fault, not a tuning problem -- check the monotonicity of every threshold against volume before shipping it.
- [2026-09-08 · Failure] ROW 0b MEASURED AND IT IS NEGATIVE: THE FLOOR IS NOT THE BOTTLENECK (2026-09-08). The scheduler now runs the consolidator every 6h, and it ran three times today, each exiting ok. New life-state feed entries produced: ZERO. The feed still holds 3 insights, all from 2026-06-18, and life_state_monitor --peek still says '(nothing to surface)'. Measured cause via consolidate.py --dry-run: window=21d turns=115 domains=5 **links=0** floor=0.6. Compare the same command on 2026-09-06: 75 turns, 5 domains, 1 link at 0.592. So the corpus grew 75 -> 115 turns and the number of candidate links went 1 -> 0. TWO CONCLUSIONS, both load-bearing. (1) The 0.60 confidence floor is NOT the binding constraint. I had framed it as 'links cross the floor as the queue grows' and expected 0.592 to rise -- instead there is now nothing at ANY confidence. Lowering the floor would surface nothing, because CrossDomainCorrelationEngine finds no cross-domain relationship to score. (2) The plumbing was never the gate. Steps 2-7 fixed the pulse (a Stop hook, then a real clock), and the pulse is now demonstrably independent of the user opening --ask -- and the organ STILL has nothing to say. stage_6_integration/ROADMAP.md 6.10 already states the correct gate: 'ambient presence is worth exactly as much as the surfacing organ has to say... build 6.10 only after the organ demonstrably produces insights worth interrupting someone for -- measured, not assumed.' It is now measured. DIRECTIVE: before any further ambient/delivery work (step 8 Telegram push included), the open question is whether domain-volume correlation is the wrong detector for 'what did I forget', not whether the transport reaches the user. Building a push channel for an organ that emits nothing would be the most expensive way yet to learn this.
- [2026-09-08 · Procedural] PYTHON GOTCHA that silently breaks log-grepping: on a str-mixin Enum, str(x) and f-string formatting DISAGREE. PermissionDecision is declared 'class PermissionDecision(str, Enum)' with DENY = 'deny'. Then: str(P.DENY) -> 'PermissionDecision.DENY' (Enum.__str__ wins), f'{P.DENY}' -> 'deny' (the str mixin supplies __format__, and f-strings call __format__ not __str__), repr(P.DENY) -> "<PermissionDecision.DENY: 'deny'>". Cost me a false test failure: the code logged the decision with an f-string ('deny') while the assertion searched for the str() form ('PermissionDecision.DENY'). The real hazard is not tests -- it is that a log line written one way and grepped the other NEVER matches, and a monitoring rule built on it silently never fires. DIRECTIVE: when logging an enum whose value must be machine-matched later, use repr() or .value explicitly, never bare interpolation. Fixed in Python 3.11+ for IntEnum via __str__ changes, but this repo runs 3.10 and the str-mixin case remains divergent regardless.
- [2026-09-08 · Failure] TWO DEFECTS IN THE SCHEDULER THAT BOTH LOOKED LIKE SUCCESS (2026-09-08, both caught by running it rather than testing it). (1) STAGGER SILENCED THE ONE-SHOT. Jobs carry initial_delay_seconds to avoid a thundering herd at daemon boot. hearth.py --tick-once builds a fresh Scheduler, so started_at is now, so EVERY job reported 'not due' -- the command printed 'ran: nothing was due' and did nothing, every time, while exiting 0. Fixed with tick(ignore_stagger=True); interval cadence still honoured so a one-shot cannot force a needless reindex. (2) A SHARED GUARD LET ONE JOB CLEAR ANOTHER'S. Both refresh_profile and reindex_memory guarded on the SAME command, scripts/check_projections.py, which exits non-zero if ANY projection is stale. Measured live: refresh_profile ran first, fixed the two projections it owns, and reindex_memory then saw exit 0 and recorded 'skipped -- guard reports nothing to do'. So the vector index would NEVER be rebuilt regardless of how far it drifted, and the skip counter would keep reporting healthy. Fixed by adding --only SUBSTRING to check_projections so each job guards on the artifact IT can fix. DIRECTIVE: a guard shared between two fixers is not a guard -- scope every conditional job to the exact artifact it repairs. And both of these are the DECORATIVE-CODE class again (tests pass, position makes it inert): the only way either surfaced was executing the real thing and reading what it actually did.
- [2026-09-08 · Failure] I SWITCHED A READER BEFORE MIGRATING ITS DATA, AND IMMEDIATELY BROKE THE GUARANTEE I WAS PROTECTING (2026-09-08, self-inflicted, caught within one command). Changing the life-state watermark from .surfaced_watermark (JSON) to .surfaced_watermark.jsonl (append-only log) was correct. But I edited the code first and left the new .jsonl EMPTY, so surfaced_ids() returned an empty set and the very next invocation re-surfaced insight 5b17b2bebd4e -- an insight already shown on 2026-06-18. That is exactly the 'NEVER NAG: each insight surfaces AT MOST ONCE, ever' guarantee the change existed to make durable. Nothing was lost (the feed was untouched and the re-surface reached me, not the user) and I folded the 3 legacy ids into the new log, which collapsed the accidental duplicate. DIRECTIVE: when relocating a store, the order is MIGRATE THE DATA, THEN SWITCH THE READER -- never the reverse. A reader pointed at an empty new location does not fail loudly; it reports 'nothing recorded yet', which is indistinguishable from a clean slate and silently voids every guarantee built on that record. Same shape as the index_memory tier-metadata bug (1030 chunks, 0 hits, looked like success).
- [2026-09-08 · Failure] THE TORN-LINE APPEND DEFECT: a flock does NOT make an append-only log safe (found 2026-09-08 by a test written for unrelated new code). A lock serialises concurrent writers. It does nothing about a writer that was KILLED mid-write, which leaves a final line with no newline terminator -- and the NEXT append then lands on that same line, so the torn record DESTROYS the good record appended after it rather than only itself. A fold over the log then loses both. Discovered because a smoke test wrote a corrupt watermark as '}{corrupt' with no trailing newline and the good event appended after it vanished; the assertion I expected to be trivially true failed. Audit result: kb_append.py has guarded this since it shipped ('Ensure we start on a fresh line even if the file lacked a trailing \n' -- seek SEEK_END, read the last byte, write \n if needed), and THREE other append-only logs had never copied it: agent/capture.py append_observation (the observation queue, written every single turn), agent/context_ledger.py archive_span (where a lost span makes an evicted context unrecoverable -- it defeats that module's entire purpose), and agent/life_state_monitor.py mark_surfaced. All three fixed; suites 18/18, 17/17, 20/20. DIRECTIVE: every append-only writer must heal a missing terminator BEFORE appending. The correct pattern already existed in this repo and three later call sites diverged from it -- when a subtle correctness guard exists in one place, grep for the other writers rather than assuming the idiom spread.
- [2026-09-08 · Failure] BOTH DEFERRED STEP-6 ITEMS RE-EXAMINED AND BOTH OF MY ORIGINAL RATIONALES WERE WRONG (2026-09-08). I had deferred content-addressed KB ids and the watermark event-fold with one sentence: 'both solve concurrent multi-writer conflicts that cannot exist until the hearth ships.' Measured before building: (1) CONTENT-ADDRESSED IDS -- the motivating L303 collision has NEVER OCCURRED. kb_append.py --audit over 558 entries with 258 explicit ids returns duplicate_ids {}. fcntl.flock plus single-user-at-a-time held for the file's whole life, and the hearth does NOT add a same-machine writer flock cannot serialise. So a risky rewrite of the widest-reach field in the repo (five consumers read id) would have prevented a failure with no instances. Replaced with a DETECTOR: projections.id_collisions() + a check_projections line. (2) WATERMARK -- mark_surfaced was ALREADY flock'd with a correct read-modify-write, so same-machine concurrency was never the hazard either. The real hazard was git: .surfaced_watermark was a TRACKED JSON object, and JSON has no union merge driver, so two laptops surfacing insights clobber each other's ids -- which breaks the never-nag guarantee this file exists to keep. Fix was smaller and better than an event-fold format: rename to .surfaced_watermark.jsonl and it inherits merge=union from .gitattributes for free. Concatenation IS merge. DIRECTIVE: 'defer until X exists' is only valid if X is VERIFIED to be the thing that creates the condition. I asserted the hearth would create the writers; flock already handled the same-machine case and git had already created the cross-machine case months earlier. Verify the precondition, not just the fix.

Raw ASGI, NOT FastAPI -- uvicorn 0.46 is already installed while fastapi/starlette are absent, so this adds ZERO dependencies, and a bare ASGI callable is testable with a fake receive/send pair with no socket at all (all 23 hearth tests run offline). Verified live: POST /v1/ask boots the real Mind, calls 4-9 tools, streams narration over SSE, and a SECOND request continued the same conversation session -- 'same mind' is now a routing property, not a sync problem. Safety posture is deliberately timid: binds 127.0.0.1 only AND re-checks the peer address per request, requires a bearer token even on loopback (loopback is not a boundary against other local processes), serialises to one request at a time with 409 rather than interleaving mutable session state, and DENIES every permission prompt while reporting the denial in the response. DIRECTIVE: --ask keeps its in-process default and gains opt-in --via-hearth, because the hearth has no TTY so it cannot honour a human gate -- routing the terminal there by default would SILENTLY REDUCE capability. That flips to default once the Commitment gate (step 10) provides remote approval.

Fixed with stable_key(): uid=1000(swara_unix) gid=1000(swara_unix) groups=1000(swara_unix),4(adm),20(dialout),24(cdrom),25(floppy),27(sudo),29(audio),30(dip),44(video),46(plugdev),117(netdev) when present, else a content hash — now 554 entries / 1030 chunks. Same silent-partial-coverage class as KB 508. VERIFIED, and the isolation matters: with ZERO tools available so nothing COULD be asked for, the assembled system prompt already contained 'tier=WARM score=0.05 id=kb-538-1' verbatim including the exact constant _DEFAULT_BUDGET_USD. Memory arrives BEFORE the model gets a turn. Wired in boot.py only when a store is open (no store -> no WARM tier -> auto-retrieve would burn a query per turn for nothing); _AUTO_RETRIEVE_TOP_K=4 because every item is a standing per-turn token tax, not a one-off. Whether it makes JARVIS say the thing you did not ask for is the measurement that remains, and index_memory.py must be RE-RUN as the KB grows or retrieval silently goes stale. DIRECTIVE: never wire auto-retrieval without first verifying the target collection contains the user's own history — an empty or wrong-corpus retriever is worse than none, because it looks like success.
- [2026-09-08 · Decision] STEP 4 SHIPPED: THE CONTEXT LEDGER — eviction is now REVERSIBLE (2026-09-08). New organs: agent/context_ledger.py (17/17 tests) + agent/tools/context.py ContextExpandTool (10/10). Compaction was a one-way door: compact.py replaced a window with a summary and persisted the originals NOWHERE. Now every span is archived VERBATIM to an append-only flock'd JSONL under jarvis_data/context/<session>.jsonl BEFORE eviction, keyed by a content-addressed handle (sha256[:16]), and the boundary message carries ctx:<handle> so the transcript itself points back at what it dropped. If the archive write FAILS the compaction is ABANDONED — a smaller prompt is not worth an unrecoverable transcript. Handles double as checksums: expand_span verifies the content still hashes to its own handle and returns None on mismatch, so corruption is detectable rather than served as plausible-looking wrong history. TWO DISCOVERIES THAT CHANGED THE DESIGN, both found by tests I wrote to prove the opposite. (1) A FORMAT MISMATCH silently disabled the whole anti-drift mechanism: as_message() emitted a BARE handle inside context_expand(...), while extract_handles() only matches the ctx: prefixed form. Writer and reader disagreed, so _resolve_originals could never find a prior boundary. Caught only because the two-pass drift test asserted on the summariser's actual INPUT rather than on a return flag. (2) THE ANTI-DRIFT LAW WAS ALREADY SATISFIED STRUCTURALLY, and my defence was aimed at the wrong risk. A boundary carries role='system' and is inserted immediately after the lead, so _split() classifies it as LEADING on the next pass and it never re-enters a compactable window — a summary can therefore never be summarised. But that exposed a REAL problem I had not designed for: boundaries then ACCUMULATE in the lead, which is never compacted, so ten compactions would leave ten permanently-resident summaries and a prompt floor that creeps up forever. Fixed by folding: _split_lead separates prior boundaries, _expand_boundaries expands their handles back to originals, and the new summary is computed from THAT union — so exactly ONE boundary survives at any time AND derivation depth stays 1. Proven over three successive compactions: boundaries stayed at 1, the third summary read ORIG-0 and contained no prior SUMMARY text, and accounting was exact — 16 archived + 2 resident = all 18 originals, zero gaps, zero double-counting. An unexpandable handle KEEPS its boundary rather than dropping the only trace of it. WIRED IN PRODUCTION: assemble_mind gained session_id, builds the ledger once and shares it between the compactor and the tool; orchestrator passes sess.session_id. Verified live: --ask now ships 6 tools including context_expand, compactor._ledger is a ContextLedger, path keyed per session. permgate allowlists context_expand as read-only (it reads one append-only file by handle and cannot write). ALSO corrected permgate's docstring, which still read 'default ALLOW for the safe majority' — accurate prose for the fail-open bug fixed in step 1, and the FIFTH instance this week of prose outliving the code it described. jarvis_data/context/ gitignored: machine-local working memory, not the mind. Regression: context_ledger 17/17, tools.context 10/10, compact 18/18, react 112/112, mind 15/15, tokens 21/21, boot 20/20, permgate 13/13, llm_client 23/23, context_injector 18/18, Final Boss 8/8. DIRECTIVE: 'infinite context' now means exactly this — nothing is unreachable, the model always knows what it is NOT looking at, and no byte is dropped without a marker saying how to get it back. Do not describe it as unbounded attention.
- [2026-09-08 · Failure] STEP 3: THE COMPACTOR WAS WIRED TO A POSITION WHERE IT COULD NOT POSSIBLY HELP (found + fixed 2026-09-08). Three defects, all verified, and the middle one is the interesting failure mode. (1) boot.py never passed compactor=, so Mind._compactor was None on every real --ask and WorkingMemoryCompactor -- 322 lines, 17 passing tests -- had NEVER executed outside its own smoke suite. (2) mind.py:191 called _maybe_compact(react) AFTER _run_react had already returned. By then every oversized request had already been sent, and the message list being compacted was about to be discarded with the run. Worse, mind.py:313 computed 'result = await compact(...)' and then returned bool(result.compacted), DROPPING result.messages. So even in its own tests the compaction was thrown away. (3) ReActLoop.__init__ had NO compactor parameter at all -- there was no seam to pass one through, so nothing could have wired it correctly without this change. THE CLASS OF BUG IS 'DECORATIVE CODE': not dead (it ran in tests, and MindResult.compacted was populated from it) but positioned so it cannot affect the outcome. That is harder to spot than dead code, because coverage and a green suite both look fine. FIX: added compactor= to ReActLoop, added ReActLoop._compact_in_place() which does 'messages[:] = result.messages' -- the assignment mind.py never made -- and called it BEFORE self._call_llm inside the iteration loop. ReActResult gained compactions:int. Mind now threads compactor= down and _maybe_compact is rewritten to a pure read (react.compactions > 0). boot.py constructs the compactor. A DESIGN ERROR I MADE AND CAUGHT MID-STEP: I first sized the compaction threshold from the model's context_length via TokenBudget. That looks right and is wrong -- google/gemini-3.6-flash reports context_length=1048576, so a window-sized threshold would never compact until ~1M tokens. OVERFLOW IS NOT THE BINDING PROBLEM; RE-BILLING IS: react.py:390 re-sends the whole transcript every iteration, so at max_iterations=40 a T-token prefix costs ~40*T. That is precisely why one hard question cost Rs72 on 2026-09-07. Corrected to a COST-driven target (_COMPACT_COST_TARGET_TOKENS = 48_000) with the real window allowed only to LOWER it -- verified: 1M-window model -> 48,000; an 8K model -> 5,952. PROVEN END TO END, asserting on the message list rather than a bool as the plan demanded: a scripted 7-iteration run with 4KB tool blobs produced compactions=4, the boundary summary present in the LIVE message list, and a correct final answer. Regression: react 112/112, mind 15/15, compact 17/17, tokens 21/21, memory_manager 51/51, boot 20/20, llm_client 23/23, permgate 13/13, context_injector 18/18, Final Boss 8/8. DIRECTIVE: when a component has passing tests but has never run in production, check WHERE it is called before assuming it works -- position is a correctness property, and 'it has tests' does not establish it.
- [2026-09-08 · Procedural] STEP 2 SHIPPED: ONE CALIBRATED TOKEN COUNTER, REPLACING TWO len//4 GUESSES (2026-09-08). New organ agent/tokens.py — TokenCounter + TokenBudget + a process-wide shared_counter(). 21/21 smoke tests. WHAT WAS WRONG: every token number in the system was len(text)//4, in TWO independent copies that could drift — brain/llm_client.py:75 (used once at :225 for the budget gate) and agent/compact.py:73 + estimate_tokens() (used at :82 and :180 for every compaction decision). Worse, NOTHING anywhere compared an assembled prompt against the target model's context_length, even though that value is loaded at router.py:241 and llm_client.pick_free_model literally SORTS on it. So the system could not answer the question that governs all paging: will this fit? WHY NOT A TOKENIZER: tiktoken is not installed; transformers is, but the catalog holds 364 models across a dozen tokenizer families and loading one per model on CPU is not viable — and it would be a DIFFERENT wrong answer, since OpenAI BPE does not tokenize Gemini or Kimi text. THE BETTER SOURCE WAS ALREADY THERE: llm_client.py:264-266 reads usage.prompt_tokens from the provider response and only falls back to the estimate. Every real call is a free labelled sample — we sent N chars, the provider counted T tokens. observe(model, chars, actual) feeds that back via EWMA (alpha 0.25), so each model's ratio converges on measured reality with zero new dependencies. Guard rails: samples under 200 chars ignored as noise, ratios outside 1.5-12.0 ignored rather than absorbed (a poisoned ratio silently breaks every paging decision). ONE BUG I INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT: observe() was wired but save() was not, so calibration lived only in memory — and since every --ask is a fresh process, the ratio would have reset to 4.0 forever and never converged. Fixed with an atexit flush (one write per process, not one per observation). LIVE RESULT, and it is honestly deflating: after one real question (5 samples) google/gemini-3.6-flash learned 3.9639 chars/token — almost exactly the old hardcoded 4.0. The old guess was fine FOR THIS MODEL ON ENGLISH PROSE. The value is not that 4.0 was wrong, it is that it is now MEASURED PER MODEL, so code-heavy, CJK and Kimi-tokenizer models will diverge visibly instead of silently. ALSO: deleted llm_client's now-unreferenced _CHARS_PER_TOKEN outright rather than leaving it with a 'kept for tests' comment — CLAUDE.md forbids exactly that shim, and I had written one before checking. token_ratios.json is gitignored as a PROJECTION (KB 548 taxonomy); syncing it would repeat the cognitive_index/chromadb mistake that hid the 544-vs-533 drift. Regression: tokens 21/21, compact 17/17, react 112/112, llm_client 23/23, boot 20/20, memory_manager 51/51, permgate 13/13, context_injector 18/18, Final Boss 8/8. DIRECTIVE: TokenBudget is now the only sanctioned way to ask 'does this fit'; do not reintroduce a local chars-per-token constant anywhere.

DIRECTIVE: this is the FOURTH instance this week of prose-declares-X / code-does-not-X (KB 521 self-describing files, KB 527 prose status headers, 2026-09-06 privacy guarantee, KB 547 the gitignored-in-comment-only settings file). In this repo a policy stated only in prose is not merely unenforced -- it is routinely FALSE. When you read a docstring asserting a safety property, VERIFY IT AGAINST A LIVE CALL before relying on it.
- [2026-09-08 · Decision] CONNECTIVITY ARCHITECTURE DECISIONS + THE STORAGE/INDEX DISTINCTION (2026-09-08, user-confirmed across two turns). FIVE DECISIONS: (1) Third-party servers may call JARVIS -- wanted as a capability, not a near-term use. Shape is a SCOPED OUTBOUND TOKEN (ask:readonly, no tools, no memory writes, no spend), rate-limited, revocable, Intent-Ledger-logged, and deliberately the LAST build step because exposing a system whose permission layer currently fails open is the one thing that must not happen early. (2) The restaurant call STAYS -- I raised the objection once, the user overruled it, AND I WAS PARTLY WRONG: my objection was US-centric (California B.O.T. Act), and India has no bot-disclosure statute for a personal agent placing a call; TRAI's UCC framework governs MARKETING calls, not this. The objection is replaced by a REQUIREMENT: a call must end in verifiable confirmation (callback, SMS, booking reference) or report failure, because a booking wrongly believed successful is worse than none. (3) Device-key storage gets an interface NOW, implemented as a plain file -- free today, expensive after devices are enrolled. Host-compromise threat model deferred by the user until JARVIS is complete. (4) Telegram now -> browser PWA -> native Android with Firebase (free) + Play Store (one-time $25). iOS DROPPED: $99/yr is 38% of the conservative annual envelope, for one user, on an envelope with no hosting line. (5) GraphRAG 4.6 PROMOTED from trigger-gated-deferred to REQUIRED. THE CONCEPTUAL CORRECTION, which is the substantive content and fixes a live bug: the user asked 'context storage is GraphRAG, what about memory storage -- SQLite?' GraphRAG is NOT storage. It is one of four INDEXES. Storage = the append-only log (knowledge_base.jsonl + the new Context Ledger), authoritative, the only thing that is true. Indexes = ChromaDB (semantic), BM25 (keyword), cognitive_index.sqlite3 (structured), GraphRAG (multi-hop) -- all derived, disposable, rebuildable. This is ENDGAME Sec4's own 4-Layer Retrieval Stack, already written; the user's instinct pointed straight at their own architecture. THE LOG MUST STAY JSONL AND MUST NOT MOVE TO SQLITE: append-only JSONL has the property that CONCATENATION IS MERGE, which is the entire basis of the multi-device coherence design; a SQLite file is a binary B-tree and two devices writing it produce a conflict git cannot merge. LIVE BUG THIS EXPLAINS: KB 544 entries vs cognitive_index 533 vs cognitive_profile header 533, with ALL FIVE artifacts git-tracked INCLUDING the derived ones -- syncing a projection as if authoritative is how a mind forks on one machine unnoticed. WHY GRAPHRAG IS NOW REQUIRED: the Context Ledger delivers unbounded RECALL, but retrieval is query-shaped and cannot serve the one case of connecting two distant facts when you did not know to look for either -- which is multi-hop, is Layer 3's stated job, and is verbatim Sec1.2's moat sentence. DIRECTIVE: never again describe GraphRAG, Chroma, BM25 or SQLite as storage; they are indexes over one append-only log.

- [2026-09-07 · Decision] PER-QUESTION SPEND CEILING REMOVED FROM --ask (2026-09-07, user directive). _DEFAULT_BUDGET_USD in brain/orchestrator.py goes 0.10 -> None. CostTracker, build_llm_call and RoutingConstraints already treated None as unbounded, so the mechanism supported this cleanly; only ONE site did arithmetic on the value -- the critic budget, round(budget_usd * 0.5) -- which now guards for None because halving None is not a smaller number, it is a crash. Type hints widened to Optional[float] at 3 orchestrator signatures plus OpenRouterTarget. THE REASON, and it is measurement not thrift: during the --ask trial a hard question (log entry 003) spent 0.074 of the 0.10 ceiling answering, and the next comparable question would have died mid-ReAct. A budget kill does not produce a cheaper answer, it produces a TRUNCATED one -- which during a trial whose whole purpose is comparing answer quality is noise injected into the data. WHAT THIS GIVES UP, recorded so nobody rediscovers it as a surprise: with budget_usd None, CostTracker.would_exceed() returns False always and the Stage-4.3.2 budget governor / downshift goes inert. A runaway ReAct loop is now bounded ONLY by max_iterations, not by spend. That is a real reduction in fail-closed protection and it was accepted deliberately. Pass --budget 0.50 to restore a ceiling for any single run; the flag still works and its help text now documents both the new default and what the default gives up. OBSERVED COSTS for calibration: trivial question 0.031 (7 calls), medium 0.055 (10 calls, continued session), hard 0.074 (11 calls). - [2026-09-07 · Decision] THE FOUR GOALS COLLAPSE INTO ONE ORGAN (2026-09-06, canon re-scoped same day). After Codex concluded JARVIS is not the vehicle for 3 of the user's 4 goals, the user asked the right follow-up -- not 'is it over' but 'how COULD each goal be achievable via JARVIS'. Interrogating that produced a convergence. THE COUNTERFACTUAL TEST, which is the only honest way to ask this: what does JARVIS give the user that paying for a frontier subscription does not? Reasoning -- no. Context -- no. Knowledge -- no. Even personalization -- no, a frontier model matches it the moment you paste the right context. EXACTLY ONE THING SURVIVES: it cannot fire when you did not know to ask. Pasting requires knowing what you forgot. THE CAPABILITY IS THEREFORE 'unprompted surfacing over your own history', and all four goals reduce to it: work leverage = 'this reverses your July call, the reason then was Y'; frontier R&D = 'you tried this optical approach in March, it failed on brightness'; learning = 'you have been confused by this concept twice before'; ambient = NOT A GOAL, it is the DELIVERY CHANNEL for the other three. Four goals -> one organ + one channel, which is dramatically smaller than the twelve-specialist roster. TWO CONSEQUENCES, both written into the canon today. (1) The organ needs NO TRAINED ADAPTER -- it runs on the KB, consolidator and retrieval, all Stage 2/3 machinery already built. So ENDGAME Sec1.1 point 1's 'trained, not injected' is DEMOTED from settled deliverable to OPEN QUESTION in stage_5_specialists/ROADMAP.md, with the prior question stated explicitly: does a trained adapter beat the already-built retrieval+surfacing path on the user's real recurring work? Nobody has tested it, testing costs nothing, and it is a strict prerequisite to the Rs1,480-2,960 Engineer run because yes and no imply different quarters. (2) ENDGAME Sec3's twelve-specialist roster is flagged as architecture-if-justified, not a build plan -- ten of twelve have been deferred since 2026-08-10 and none has moved. (3) stage_6's 6.10 ambient is reframed as delivery channel with a hard ordering: ambient is worth exactly as much as the organ has to say, so build it only after the organ demonstrably produces insights worth interrupting someone for. DIRECTIVE: when asked what JARVIS is for, lead with unprompted surfacing, not with the roster, the adapter, or corpus depth. And do not cite roster completeness as a reason to build anything.

They stated a half-remembered result ('some particles like electrons already know how we're going to measure them and they change themselves accordingly before hand'), then DERIVED AN ONTOLOGICAL CONSEQUENCE FROM IT and proposed a specific testable geometry: 'this means time is not linear, time should be a vertical line going above and below endlessly with the middle being the exact present.' WHAT I CORRECTED: measurement is coupling not observation (the interference term is scaled by environment distinguishability, CONTINUOUSLY -- a partial detector gives partial fringes, which kills 'the particle decides to be a wave or particle'); delayed choice rewrites nothing because no fact existed to rewrite; the quantum eraser's fringes are a SORTING of already-fixed screen data, not a retroactive change -- and the raw screen data never shows fringes at all. NOTE ON MY OWN ANSWER, worth flagging: I connected the eraser's post-selection artifact to survivorship bias, which is the same objection I raised against their historical-archetype sample on 2026-08-24. WHAT IT RETROACTIVELY EXPLAINS: several facts already in the KB read as independent until this row and now resolve to one cause -- the lapsed athletic peak (90kg bench at 66kg bw, stated entirely in past tense), the displaced hobby projects, the 'no-WLB service job leaves no discretionary time' constraint that shaped the specialist-roster scoping, and the month-block planning horizon (KB pattern: planning-horizon). The job did not merely crowd out hobbies; it displaced a real training practice. SECOND-ORDER OBSERVATION WORTH HOLDING CAREFULLY: the AVP appreciation -- the strongest professional recognition on record -- was EARNED BY delivering under exactly the conditions that cost them the practice. Reward and cost share a source. That is not a conclusion about what they should do; it is a tension to be aware of before offering advice that assumes the two can be separated. DIRECTIVE: do not treat 'extreme deadline' as ambient background when it appears in their accounts. It has a named, repeating, upstream origin the user has already diagnosed. And when scoping any JARVIS work, the discretionary-time budget is genuinely small -- prefer plans that survive being interrupted for weeks over plans that need consecutive evenings.
- [2026-09-03 · Failure] SELF-DESCRIBING FILES WENT STALE AND BECAME TRAINING DATA (found + fixed 2026-09-03). Two files carried headers asserting their own git status: personal_life.md said 'LOCAL ONLY. Not git-tracked' and experience_map.md said 'GITIGNORED, deliberately'. Both were TRACKED, committed and pushed -- git check-ignore exits 1 on both. They were true at creation and invalidated by the 2026-08-26 everything-in-git decision, which changed .gitignore but not the prose inside the files it stopped excluding. WHY THIS IS NOT COSMETIC: personal_life.md is ingested WHOLE by personalization_corpus.py iter_personal_life_records, so its header IS chunk 0 of the corpus -- the training set was teaching the model a false statement about where its own memory lives. A self-describing file that is also training data has a second failure mode ordinary docs do not: staleness propagates into weights. THE GENERAL RULE: a claim a file makes ABOUT ITSELF (tracked/ignored/local/synced/deprecated) has no owner and no trigger to re-check, exactly like the absence-has-no-event problem in KB 496. When a policy file (.gitignore, config, roadmap status) changes, grep the files it governs for prose asserting the old policy. Fixed by rewriting both headers to state the true status while PRESERVING the reasoning as history -- experience_map's own header had actually named the condition ('flip it to tracked once the repo is private'), which had been met and never actioned. Corpus regenerated: personal_life 1 -> 2 records, blend 3,127 records / 13.4% personalization char share. SECOND FINDING, same audit, worth its own attention: of 795,386 chars in personalization_corpus, ~40% (kb_identity + kb_judgment + personal_life) is MY prose ABOUT the user, not the user's words. Purely-authored-by-them material (literature) is 2.1%. user_voice is 16.5% and 44% of it is fragments. The corpus that is supposed to carry their voice is mostly my description of their voice -- which is why SFT_SPEC's 200 (question -> their own long answer) pairs, sourced from conversation_topics.md section D, are the scarce input rather than more volume.
- [2026-09-02 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: probes_confidence_before_spending_attention (2026-09-02). Before asking for a large concrete deliverable -- sample output tables for five Databricks metadata commands -- the user prefixed it: 'IF YOU ARE SURE, can you provide sample tables...'. This is a distinct move from recall_probe_before_load (which tests retained context) and from preempts_the_known_answer (which fences off the floor of an answer). Here they are gating on MY CALIBRATION before committing their own reading time to output they cannot cheaply verify -- sample table schemas are exactly the kind of artifact where a confident hallucination is expensive and invisible. It paid off immediately: the honest answer was that one of the five commands they named ('DESCRIBE DETAIL EXTENDED') does not exist, and that a second ('DESCRIBE DETAIL') returns exactly one row so their '2-3 rows each' framing could not apply. Both corrections were only surfaceable because the question licensed 'no'. DIRECTIVE: treat 'if you are sure' as a real request for a confidence boundary, never as rhetorical warmth. Answer it explicitly and BEFORE the deliverable: state what is verified, what is constructed from spec, what is version-dependent, and what could not be checked in this environment (here: the Databricks MCP connector was down with a 404, so nothing could be executed). Volunteering the unverifiable parts is what makes the verified parts worth reading. This composes with their own recorded engineering rule -- 'guard against confident wrong answer harder than against crash' -- so a hedge is not weakness to this user, it is the requested output.

CITATION CONVENTION that falls out of this, now written into RSA_Answer_Bank.md Part 3: cite the LIVE session_learnings by SECTION ANCHOR (line numbers rot in a file still being appended to through Dec) and the FROZEN Data_Engineering_Lessons.md by line number.
