- [2026-05-21 · System_Protocol] When working-tree contains modified or new learning artifacts (KB entries, jarvis_data/ markdown files for ANY domain — JARVIS roadmap, Data Engineering, Finance, literature, future domains — or jarvis_core/ code), JARVIS MUST auto-push on detection. Do NOT defer waiting for explicit 'push it' instruction. Do NOT bundle-defer ('separate commit later') under bundling-discipline pretense. Rule applies even when current task is unrelated (e.g., cleanup turn detects pending DE entries). Surface what's being pushed in chat (migration manifest) but EXECUTE the push. Single exception: binary regenerables already in .gitignore (chromadb/, __pycache__/, .bak files, third-party clones). Rationale: learnings stranded on one laptop are THE failure mode JARVIS exists to prevent; the sync gap is more costly than slightly messier commit history.
- [2026-05-19 · Decision] NEW Utility Library: temporal_resolver (Stage 3.2 ships alongside Tool ABC + memory tools). Pure-functional library used BY temporal_grounding middleware (Stage 3.4) and optionally callable from agent (Stage 3.2 tools/cognitive.py addition). NOT a Category A/B/C primary — shared utility, analogous to how jarvis_core/memory/chunking.py is to ingestion. FILE: jarvis_core/agent/temporal.py (~400 lines target). PUBLIC API (pure functions, no LLM call, no network): (1) resolve_relative_date(expr: str, reference: datetime, locale: str = 'en-IN') -> Optional[datetime]: parses 'yesterday', 'last week', '3 days ago', 'this morning', 'Monday', 'before lunch' etc. Returns None on unparseable. Uses dateparser library + custom Indian-English regex extensions (lunch/dinner/IST 9-5). (2) resolve_event_reference(expr: str, git_log: Sequence[Commit], kb_index: Sequence[KBEntry]) -> List[ResolvedRef]: parses 'before X was pushed', 'since the feature landed', 'after the rerank fix'. Searches commit messages + KB content via fuzzy match (rapidfuzz, ratio>=70); returns ranked candidates with confidence 0.0-1.0. (3) resolve_pronoun(expr: str, conversation: Sequence[Message]) -> Optional[ResolvedRef]: resolves 'the test', 'that bug', 'the fix' against recent conversation; last-mention + coreference heuristics. (4) detect_temporal_markers(text: str) -> List[Marker]: dispatcher — returns [(start_idx, end_idx, marker_type, raw_text)] used to route to (1)/(2)/(3) per marker_type. DATA TYPES: @dataclass(frozen=True) Marker, ResolvedRef, Commit (lightweight git log shim — sha, timestamp, message, modified_paths). TEST COVERAGE: 100% pure-function smoke tests in __main__ block (per Code Style rule, no separate test file). 50+ test cases: explicit dates × 10, relative refs × 15, event refs × 15, pronouns × 10. FALLBACK BEHAVIOR: if all four return None for a marker, temporal_grounding middleware logs the unresolved marker to jarvis_data/unresolved_temporal_markers.jsonl for offline pattern expansion. LLM-based ambiguity-resolution lives IN THE MIDDLEWARE, not the resolver — resolver stays deterministic + pure. DEPENDENCIES: dateparser (pip), rapidfuzz (pip); both Apache 2.0, CPU-only, no GPU required. DIRECTIVE: land at Stage 3.2 entry sprint alongside cognitive_mirror callable + writing_voice_check (they share the 'resolve user-language to KB references' pattern). ~1 day of work — small, deterministic, smoke-tested. Becomes load-bearing at Stage 3.4 when temporal_grounding ships.
- [2026-05-19 · Decision] NEW 6th Stage-3.5 Category-C daemon: git_kb_correlator (AUGMENTS Decision 2026-05-19 Daemon Inventory L285; slots alongside session_resume_context + failure_pattern_alarm + narrate_progress + cross_runtime_sync_check + attention_handoff on the MemGPT heartbeat backbone). Pre-computes the correlation index that temporal_grounding middleware queries at request time. Keeps middleware p95 <100ms by avoiding live git+KB scan per request. TRIGGERS: (a) post-commit git hook on every commit to main (primary); (b) full backfill on first run (walks all commits since 2026-03-01); (c) hourly cron belt-and-suspenders catch-up. MECHANISM: for each commit C with modified_paths P[]: (1) walks KB entries in last 7 days; (2) for each entry E, checks if E.content mentions any file / feature name / symbol in P[] (substring + fuzzy match); (3) records correlation: {kb_timestamp, commit_sha, modified_paths, correlation_score 0.0-1.0 (path-mention strength), evidence_substring (the matching span)}. OUTPUT FILE: jarvis_data/git_kb_correlations.jsonl (GITIGNORED — derivable index, treated as regenerable like ChromaDB; rebuild via --backfill). Schema per line: {kb_timestamp: ISO, commit_sha: str, modified_paths: List[str], correlation_score: float, evidence: str, indexed_at: ISO}. Append-only; --backfill flag rebuilds from scratch. SCHEMA-PORT: NOT loaded into main KB — KB stays semantic insights; this is structural correlation index. Add to .gitignore alongside chromadb/. PERFORMANCE BUDGET: post-commit hook <1s p95 (most commits touch <5 files, <20 KB entries in scan window); backfill <60s for typical 200 commits + 300 KB entries. FAILURE MODES: (a) corrupt index -> temporal_grounding falls back to live git log + KB scan (slow but correct); (b) commit during indexing -> defer to next hook fire (idempotent); (c) symbol-mention ambiguity -> record all candidates with low confidence, middleware disambiguates; (d) KB compaction shifts line numbers -> index uses timestamps as IDs, not line numbers, so compaction-stable. DIRECTIVE: install post-commit git hook at Stage 3.5 entry alongside MemGPT heartbeat; full backfill is the activation gate. After 3.5 ships, temporal_grounding middleware switches from live-scan (slow) to index-lookup (fast) automatically via fallback chain.
- [2026-05-19 · Decision] NEW 8th Category-B middleware: temporal_grounding (AUGMENTS Decision 2026-05-19 Category B Inventory). Closes the Iron-Man 'ambient time-awareness' gap — JARVIS knows WHEN code fired without procedural CoT reasoning. Eliminates the failure mode where Claude/JARVIS iteratively debugs differences that are trivially explained by 'a commit happened between then and now'. HOOK: PRE_PROMPT (fires before LLM sees user message). INPUTS: current user message, last N=20 conversation turns, KB entries from last 90 days, git log (full), telemetry gap_seconds + local_hour. ALGORITHM: (1) regex + spaCy NER scan for temporal/causal markers — explicit dates ('yesterday' / 'last week'), relative refs ('before X' / 'after Y' / 'since Z'), pronouns referring to past events ('the test' / 'that bug' / 'the fix'), causal language ('because' / 'after' / 'before'); (2) for each marker, call temporal_resolver utility (jarvis_core/agent/temporal.py, Stage 3.2 lib) to resolve to specific timestamp / commit SHA / KB entry; (3) build TEMPORAL_CONTEXT block: per-ref resolution + relevant KB excerpts + commit summaries + causal frame; (4) inject block into system prompt BEFORE user message. OUTPUT SCHEMA: 'TEMPORAL_CONTEXT (auto-resolved):' followed by per-ref lines 'User mention X -> resolved Y (timestamp T, commit SHA, KB entries [L_a, L_b])'; then causal-frame summary. COMPOSITION ORDER: fires FIRST in middleware chain — BEFORE cognitive_mirror (temporal context informs which Cognitive_Patterns are relevant). PERFORMANCE BUDGET: <100ms p95 (typical <5 refs/message), <500ms p99 (>10 refs); per-session resolution cache. FAILURE MODES: (a) ambiguous ref ('the bug' with 3 candidates) -> include all with confidence scores, let LLM disambiguate via context; (b) no markers found -> silent passthrough (no injection); (c) git log >10k commits -> sliding window last 1000 commits; (d) corrupt git_kb_correlator index -> fall back to live scan (slower but correct). ORDERING NOTE: Stage 3.4 ships BEFORE Stage 3.5 — temporal_grounding initially runs live scan; git_kb_correlator (Stage 3.5) makes it fast retroactively. DIRECTIVE: this is the SINGLE highest-leverage interceptor for Iron-Man feel — every response with temporal reasoning saves 5-15 tokens of CoT and avoids procedural-reasoning-arrives-at-wrong-conclusion. NEVER ship Stage 3.4 without this.
- [2026-05-19 · Decision] Category-C (Daemon/Ambient) Inventory. Runs independently of agent invocation via background scheduler. STAGE 3.5 MINIMAL BACKBONE (piggyback on MemGPT heartbeat from Decision 2026-05-13 metacognitive integration): FIVE DAEMONS register as @heartbeat_callback against the async heartbeat event loop already required for 3.5 sleep-time consolidation. (1) session_resume_context - trigger: session-start; returns continuity context (last stage, last decision, uncommitted state, recent commits from both laptops). (2) failure_pattern_alarm - trigger: error-trace-pattern-match against stored Failure entries; surfaces unprompted when current state resembles known failure. (3) narrate_progress - trigger: end-of-session OR N-hour interval; LLM-dictates 'what we did today' as Episodic KB entry (tagged heartbeat-emitted per Decision 2026-05-13 to bypass kb_compact structural rule). (4) cross_runtime_sync_check - trigger: hourly cron; detects work-vs-personal laptop divergence (uncommitted files, branch divergence, KB merge=union artifacts). (5) attention_handoff - trigger: laptop-switch detected (file mtime delta + git pull event); warm-transfers active file, lesson, last 5 chat turns. STAGE 6 FULL SCHEDULER (lands with TaskScheduler from OpenJarvis STEAL #4): ELEVEN DAEMONS. (6) world_state_briefing - daily 8 AM IST cron: calendar + market open + filtered news + GitHub + portfolio overnight P&L + active deadline flags. (7) decision_deadline_tracker - daily 9 AM IST cron: parses Finance/strategy.md triggers + KB Decisions with explicit dates; 7d/1d/day-of nudges. (8) trigger_monitor daemon form - hourly cron during market hours; watches strategy.md triggers vs live market data. (9) pre_emptive_research - trigger: topic mention in active conversation; background Tavily + KB + arxiv fetch, cached. (10) screen_observer - trigger: screenshot interval (privacy-gated per-app allowlist); multimodal description fed to KB. (11) voice_command - trigger: microphone keyword; Whisper STT -> action dispatch. (12) stage_gate_check daemon - trigger: pre-commit git hook + roadmap drift detection (any code change touching js-development/ triggers stage-compatibility audit). (13) shadow_search - trigger: any external LLM query; runs same query against JARVIS memory + public web + frontier API; logs delta. (14) unsent_thoughts_journal - trigger: explicit user invocation + periodic resurface prompt (90-day-old entries). (15) bond_check - trigger: explicit query OR weekly cron; demonstrates accumulated knowledge across full KB. (16) post_market_hook - pre/post-market wrap for after-hours surveillance. DIRECTIVE: daemon backbone MUST land at Stage 3.5 alongside MemGPT heartbeat - they share infrastructure. Pulling 5 daemons forward gives 'JARVIS feels alive' moment by week 6 instead of waiting for Stage 6. The remaining 11 land naturally when TaskScheduler ships at Stage 6.
- [2026-05-19 · Decision] Stage 3.4 Category-B (Middleware) Inventory. Interceptors fire automatically in the ReAct pipeline; NOT in Tool registry; cannot be skipped by LLM. Each registers against a hook point. SEVEN INTERCEPTORS: (1) voice_persona - hook: post-draft-pre-user-output; rewrites style to JARVIS voice calibrated on Cognitive_Pattern L265 aesthetic_compression_cross_domain; NOT British-sass, compressed minimalism per user identity. (2) cognitive_mirror middleware form - hook: pre-system-prompt-assembly; injects top-3 relevant DIRECTIVE-tagged Cognitive_Patterns into system prompt based on current user input. Different from callable form which is on-demand exploration; middleware form fires automatically every prompt. (3) integrity_check - hook: pre-high-impact-action-commit; compares proposed action against philosophical axioms (L267 unseen_grave_fear, L268 insufficiency_as_sin, L269 visceral_imagination, L270 ambition_over_passion). Returns: congruent | deviation: REASON. Blocks commit until acknowledged. (4) leak_check - hook: pre-external-LLM-call; scans prompt for sensitive markers (finance numbers, PII, KB entries tagged private). Returns: safe | REDACT: [items]. Hard-blocks send on REDACT. (5) response_calibration - hook: post-draft; checks JARVIS draft against user style rules (defines jargon, concrete examples, no sycophancy, no fluff). Returns specific violations for voice_persona to act on. (6) flow_state_detector - already shipped in 3.1.7; in 3.4 it auto-fires every N turns and feeds output to voice_persona for tone calibration (Flow->compress, Fatigue->maintain depth without simplification). (7) pattern_witness - hook: session-end; summarizes which Cognitive_Patterns fired, which were validated, which contradicted observed behavior (flagging stale patterns for update). DIRECTIVE: middleware hook framework lands as part of 3.4 ReAct loop design - extend the trace pipeline (STEAL #5 TraceStep + EventBus) with @middleware decorator + hook_point enum (PRE_PROMPT, PRE_LLM, PRE_TOOL, POST_TOOL, POST_DRAFT, PRE_OUTPUT, SESSION_END). Each interceptor registers against hook_point at import time, similar to Tool.register() pattern. Composition order: voice_persona AFTER response_calibration AFTER pattern_witness (cleanup -> calibration -> voice).

Foundation tools registered via Tool.register() with Pydantic ToolInput schemas + ToolResult dataclass + is_concurrency_safe flag. NON-MEMORY BASE (5): calculator (safe), web_search (safe, duckduckgo-search lib, no key needed), file_read (safe, 1MB cap), code_exec (UNSAFE, requires_permission=True), shell_run (UNSAFE, requires_permission=True). COGNITIVE/IDENTITY (4 callable forms): cognitive_mirror (filters KB for type=Cognitive_Pattern + DIRECTIVE tag against current context), prior_self_consult (KB query in time-window), bear_case_devil (contrarian prompt over position thesis), writing_voice_check (compare text vs user Substack corpus + KB style patterns L265). FINANCE CALLABLE (3): portfolio_state (Groww + INDmoney + bank aggregator), trigger_monitor callable form (parse Finance/strategy.md + check current market state vs triggers), incentive_planner (pre-allocate known inflows per strategy.md). STAGE 4+ CALLABLE (4): pre_mortem (3 failure modes for any plan), wargame_market (Monte Carlo portfolio scenarios), multi_specialist_consult (parallel adapter dispatch with Aggregator), agent_swarm (N parallel sub-agents on solution branches). STAGE 5+ MOONSHOT (2): patent_landscape (hologram IP search across Google Patents + arxiv + IEEE), materials_scout (MatSciBERT + papers). STAGE 6+ ACTION (3): email_compose_send (Gmail API, drafts in user voice via writing_voice_check), code_deploy (CI + RunPod push), vision_tool (Qwen2.5-VL via OpenRouter, local later). DIRECTIVE: ABC change - add Tool.requires_permission: ClassVar[bool] = False class attribute now (single change, free hook for STEAL #9 permission engine at 3.4). File layout: jarvis_core/agent/tools/{memory.py, calc.py, web.py, exec.py, fs.py, shell.py, cognitive.py, finance.py}. Memory tools share injected JarvisMemoryStore via __init__ DI; tools requiring LLM accept Callable[[str], str] via DI (NEVER import client). Total Stage 3.2 immediate: 11 base + 8 cognitive/finance = 19 callable; with Stage 4+/5+/6+ stretch: ~28 total in inventory.
- [2026-05-19 · Decision] Tool Architecture 3-Category Taxonomy (foundational framing for Stage 3 build). Confirms Iron-Man-like behavior comes 80% from B+C, 20% from A. CATEGORY A (Callable Tool, Stage 3.2): registered via Tool.register(), invoked when LLM emits {tool, input} JSON, dispatched through dispatch_batch() concurrency partition (STEAL #8). Examples: memory_*, calculator, web_search, code_exec, file_read, shell_run, cognitive_mirror callable form, bear_case_devil, portfolio_state, trigger_monitor callable form, pre_mortem, multi_specialist_consult. CATEGORY B (Middleware/Interceptor, Stage 3.4 ReAct pipeline): fires automatically on every relevant agent step, NOT in tool registry, cannot be skipped by LLM. Hooks: pre-system-prompt, pre-LLM-call, pre-tool-dispatch, post-tool-result, pre-response-finalize. Examples: voice_persona (every output), cognitive_mirror middleware form (pre-prompt), integrity_check (pre-commit), leak_check (pre-cloud-call), response_calibration (post-draft), flow_state_detector (every N turns), pattern_witness (session-end). CATEGORY C (Daemon/Ambient): runs independently of agent invocation via TaskScheduler tick loop (OpenJarvis STEAL #4). Triggers: cron, file-change, session-boundary, error-trace-pattern-match, laptop-switch. Stage 3.5 minimal backbone piggybacks on MemGPT heartbeat (session_resume_context, failure_pattern_alarm, narrate_progress, cross_runtime_sync_check, attention_handoff). Stage 6 full scheduler adds world_state_briefing, decision_deadline_tracker, trigger_monitor daemon, screen_observer, voice_command, stage_gate_check pre-commit hook, shadow_search, etc. DIRECTIVE: when describing any Stage 3+ feature, explicitly classify A/B/C. Conflating callable tools with daemons obscures WHEN code fires - the primary source of architectural bugs. Iron-Man feel = B + C, not just A.

- [2026-05-16 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: conviction_to_action. When presented with UTI Nifty Next 50 recommendation at 2.5K/mo, user immediately went and started a 4.5K/mo SIP (nearly 2x the suggestion). Then independently redistributed the remaining budget (reduced liquid fund from 5K to 3K) to fund the increase. Shows extremely high execution velocity once convinced, with a tendency to go larger than recommended. This is the same pattern as the NVIDIA initial lump sum (invested sisters 25K alongside own money immediately). DIRECTIVE: when making financial suggestions, be aware user will likely amplify the recommendation. State the max-safe allocation alongside the recommendation to prevent over-concentration.
- [2026-05-16 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: thesis_drift_tolerance. RIL position started as an event play (Jio IPO catalyst). When pressed, user expanded the thesis to 3 scenarios: (1) Jio IPO pop, (2) analyst price target 1900-2000, (3) long-term AI infrastructure hold. These are three structurally different strategies requiring different position sizes and time horizons. The user is comfortable holding positions without a single clear exit thesis, allowing narrative to evolve to justify the hold. This is how conviction positions silently become bagholding positions. Not necessarily wrong for RIL specifically (thesis has merit), but the PATTERN of thesis expansion under drawdown pressure is a risk signal. DIRECTIVE: when user discusses existing holdings that are underwater, check if the current thesis matches the original entry thesis documented in strategy.md. If they have diverged, flag it explicitly.
- [2026-05-16 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: market_timing_bias. User proposed stopping all SIPs and hoarding cash (12.5K/mo in savings account) to wait for a market crash to buy NVIDIA cheap. Classic behavioral finance trap: cash-hoarding dressed as prudence. When presented with quantified opportunity cost (12-18K/yr lost returns at 3.5% savings vs 15% equity) and the behavioral evidence (people who sat in cash during 2023-2024 missed 50%+ rally; fear peaks at exactly the moment valuations are lowest), user accepted the argument and agreed to maintain SIP discipline. DIRECTIVE: if user proposes similar timing strategies in future, do not re-argue from scratch. Reference this pattern and state: SIP IS the crash-buying mechanism. One firm pushback with data is sufficient per nag_pushback protocol (Cognitive_Pattern 2026-05-13). The user is receptive to evidence-based pushback but defaults to intuitive buy-low thinking under market stress.

- [2026-05-13 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: nag_pushback. User pushed back when foundation-gap concern (emergency fund + health insurance) was raised three times. Their counter-context held up (parents are independent and financially strong → family backstop substitutes for personal emergency fund at this stage). The nag itself was the failure, not the absence of foundation. DIRECTIVE: critical foundation gaps get raised ONCE with strong reasoning. If user provides counter-context that withstands scrutiny, concede the point. Don't re-raise unless new evidence emerges that changes the picture. The user values being heard the first time more than being protected from a low-probability risk.
- [2026-05-13 · Cognitive_Pattern] PATTERN: horizon_shift. Mid-conversation, user shifted framing from '10-20-30 year compound horizon' to 'max profits in 1-2 years' for the same portfolio. The two framings produce contradictory decisions (long-horizon → diversify, accept variance; short-horizon → concentrate, time the cycle). User wasn't aware they were switching modes. DIRECTIVE: when user reframes a previously-discussed decision with a new timeframe, flag the shift explicitly and ask which horizon is canonical for the decision before answering. Especially: 'max profits in [short window]' framing pushes user toward concentration + market-timing — three habits inconsistent with their stated 20-year strategy.

- [2026-05-13 · Decision] STEAL #10 (Stage 3.4, paired with STEAL #9): Port OpenClaude bash AST safe-command classifier. Source: src/tools/BashTool/bashPermissions.ts. Mechanism: parses bash AST (not just regex on command string), auto-approves safe ops (grep, cat, ls, head, tail, wc, find, awk read-only, sed -n print-only) BEFORE permission UX prompt fires. JARVIS application: jarvis_core/agent/permissions.py BashClassifier hook. Use Python bashlex library for AST parse (or shlex + manual structural rules if bashlex too heavy). Effort: 2 days. - [2026-05-13 · Decision] STEAL #9 (Stage 3.4 safety layer): Port OpenClaude hook-driven permission engine. Sources: src/hooks/toolPermission/PermissionContext.ts + src/utils/permissions/PermissionRule.ts + src/hooks/toolPermission/handlers/interactiveHandler.ts. Mechanism: declarative allow/ask/deny rules with wildcard + regex matching, loaded from settings JSON at startup. Async classifiers per tool. Plan-mode equivalent gate (EnterPlanModeTool). Per-tool toolPermissionContext declares behavior. JARVIS application: jarvis_core/agent/permissions.py — schema + rule matcher + interactive CLI prompt fallback when ask fires. CRITICAL safety layer between LLM tool-call emission and tool invocation, especially for destructive tools (Bash, Write, Edit). Effort: 3-4 days (schema + matcher + interactive prompt + tests). Mechanism: each Tool self-declares is_concurrency_safe() predicate; orchestrator partitions tool-calls per ReAct turn — read-only tools (memory retrieval, web search, file read) batch via asyncio.gather (parallel), stateful tools (Write, Edit, Bash) execute serial. Approx 50% latency reduction on multi-tool turns (e.g., simultaneous BM25 + semantic retrieval + cross-encoder rerank). JARVIS application: jarvis_core/agent/react.py — partition step between LLM tool-call emission and tool dispatch loop. Effort: 1-2 days. - [2026-05-13 · Decision] Metacognitive review (jarvis_data/Metacognitive_Daemon_Review.md, 2026-05-09, YELLOW verdict) NOW-phase items absorb INTO Stage 3 sub-phases as the agent framework is built from scratch (per Decision 2026-05-13 reversal of 177/222), NOT as a separate parallel sprint. Integration mapping: (3.1 Function Calling & Structured Output) Cognitive_State_Update Pydantic schema + TextTelemetry dataclass (prompt_brevity, typo_density, correction_rate, rephrasing, sentiment_shift) land alongside other constrained-generation schemas — natural slot because 3.1 already introduces outlines/Pydantic for tool-call typing; (3.4 ReAct Pattern) MIRROR-lite as single-pass system prompt template ('evaluate Goals/Reasoning/Memory before responding') injected into ReAct loop — zero extra inference cost, plus CoT loop detector via regex on <think> tag content (transition tokens: wait/alternatively/re-evaluate, frequency > 2 = flag) in the trace-logging path; (3.5 Memory-Augmented Agents/MemGPT) heartbeat flag on tool calls + async heartbeat event loop + sleep-time consolidation agent = the MemGPT page-out path itself — these aren't additions, they ARE MemGPT. DEFER unchanged from review: Stage 4 (Orchestration) gets full Meta-R1 thinking-token monitor + semantic entropy confidence scoring + veto gate w/ 3s timeout; Stage 5 (Specialists) gets real MIRROR parallel threads via Kimi adapter swap + Quiet-STaR if fine-tuning supports custom thought tokens; Stage 6 (Interface) gets voice telemetry — MFCC, speaker baselines, attention-weighted multimodal fusion. NEW DESIGN CONSTRAINT for Stage 3.5 sleep-time consolidation: heartbeat-emitted Cognitive_Pattern entries MUST carry tag 'heartbeat-emitted'; kb_compact.py MUST exclude entries with that tag from structural-rule displacement (without exclusion, 5 consecutive heartbeats observing 'user in Flow state' cluster together and get pruned as near-duplicates, destroying legitimate transient state observations). A separate stream-compaction process rolls heartbeat entries up per session into one summary (treat like log rotation, not like dedup). Compactor handles insights (Decision/Failure/Cognitive_Pattern stable patterns), stream-compactor handles state observations (heartbeat-emitted Cognitive_Pattern) — different policies, different lifetimes. Effort estimate: Cognitive_State_Update + TextTelemetry schemas ~1 day; MIRROR-lite prompt + CoT regex ~1 day inside 3.4; heartbeat loop + sleep-time consolidation ~1-2 weeks as part of 3.5; kb_compact.py exclusion rule ~2 hours follow-up patch to scripts/kb_compact.py (filter by `'heartbeat-emitted' in entry.tags` before structural-rule pass). DIRECTIVE: when reaching 3.5 implementation, BEFORE writing the heartbeat loop, patch kb_compact.py with the exclusion rule + write a stream-compactor stub so the compaction policy is in place before writes start.
- [2026-05-13 · Decision] Reverses Decisions 177 (2026-05-01) + 222 (2026-05-03) OpenClaude delegation. New direction: build jarvis_core/agent/ from scratch per master roadmap original 5 sub-phases (3.1 Function Calling + outlines, 3.2 Tool Registry, 3.3 Planning, 3.4 ReAct loop, 3.5 MemGPT). Rationale: (1) OpenClaude IS Claude Code runtime hosted by Anthropic; every query routes to Anthropic servers, contradicts 'private AI' identity in JARVIS_ENDGAME.md Section 1; (2) per-token API forever breaks fixed-training-cost + variable-compute economic model from Decision 213/217 (the same economic argument that rejected frontier APIs as the Brain default); (3) 12-specialist routing + QLoRA adapter swap + cold-wake patterns aren't first-class concepts in OpenClaude, would require wrappers that defeat the time-savings the original delegation was reasoned on; (4) Stages 3.1-3.5 in master roadmap are the canonical learning arc; OpenClaude was a 2-3 week detour. Build timeline: 10-14 weeks for Stage 3 deliverable, compressed via OpenJarvis STEAL targets (Apache 2.0 source-copy): STEAL #1 RegistryBase -> 3.2; STEAL #2 PRICING dict -> 3.2 concurrent; STEAL #5 TraceStep+EventBus -> 3.4 trace path; STEAL #6 EvalRecord framework -> 3.4 + 3.5 gates. Net delay vs OpenClaude path: 8-12 weeks on 9-15 month horizon, acceptable. CASCADES: (a) Decision 222 'KILL OpenClaude memory subsystem' directive moot (no longer using OpenClaude as runtime); (b) MCP bridge becomes optional Stage 5+ work for *publishing* JARVIS Memory to external tools (Claude Desktop, Antigravity, third-party agents) — NOT the agent runtime; (c) STAGE_3_OPENCLAUDE_STRATEGY.md to be marked SUPERSEDED 2026-05-13; (d) Master roadmap Stage 3 reverts to original 5 sub-phases (follow-up commit); (e) JARVIS_ANALYSIS.md bear-case 'OpenClaude single-maintainer risk' neutralized; (f) jarvis_core/brain/ note in Decision 177 ('NOT re-implemented in jarvis_core/brain/') reversed — jarvis_core/agent/ becomes the canonical location for ReAct, Plan, Tool, Memory-augmented patterns. DIRECTIVE: Stage 3 entry sprint = jarvis_core/agent/registry.py (STEAL #1) + cost.py (STEAL #2) + tool.py (Tool ABC) as concurrent first landings, before 3.1 Function Calling lesson.
- [2026-05-06 · Decision] STEAL #1 (Build Now, Stage 3): Port OpenJarvis RegistryBase[T] decorator pattern to jarvis_core/agent/registry.py. Source: OpenJarvis/src/openjarvis/core/registry.py:19-172. Mechanism: generic base class with per-subclass isolation via dynamic attribute names (`_registry_entries_{ClassName}`); decorator `@AgentRegistry.register("key")` populates dict at import time; lookup is O(1); instantiation via `create(key, *args, **kwargs)`. Same shape reused across Tool/Engine/Agent/Channel/Memory registries in OpenJarvis. JARVIS application: Stage 4.A specialist loading (router resolves specialist by ID at runtime) + Stage 5 QLoRA adapter swap (pre-register all 12 adapters at boot, swap their internal state via registry lookup, NOT registry mutation since registration is import-time only). Effort: ~1 day, low complexity. DIRECTIVE: Land this in the Stage 3 entry commit alongside mcp_bridge.py so the bridge can register Memory tools via the registry from day one rather than retrofit. Caveat: registries are import-time only — no dynamic unload. For runtime hot-swap, use registry-lookup + state-mutation pattern, not registry-mutation.

Add RunPod GPU-hour pricing entries (₹91/hr for 4× A5000 INT4, ₹37/hr for A40 + CPU offload, ₹117/hr for A100 — per JARVIS_ENDGAME.md Section 2). Replace memory_backend with JARVIS Memory stack (store/expansion/compression/bm25/hybrid/rerank); replace operator_id with specialist_id (e.g., "financier_lora_v1"); use JSONL+ChromaDB for session_store, NOT OpenJarvis's SQLite SessionStore (already rejected in master verdict line 223).

JARVIS application: Stage 6 cron-triggered Finance/hologram outer daemon — wraps the MonitorOperative-state-pattern cold-wake (STEAL #3) with the actual scheduling/triggering logic. NSE market-close trigger -> daemon ticks every N min during market hours -> trigger evaluator (price spikes, bulk-deal anomalies) -> if trigger fires, call cold-wake-Financier(state). Effort: low, ~1-2 days, mostly direct copy with EventBus integration. DIRECTIVE: Pair with STEAL #3 — together they are the agentic infrastructure (cron + ingestion + triggers + cold-wake + notifier) called out as Stage 6 work in Decision line 217. Land both in same Stage 6 sprint.

- [2026-05-03 · Decision] Architecture Review Decision (2026-05-03): MCP bridge approach is GREEN with 3 operational risks. DIRECTIVES: (1) KILL OpenClaude memory subsystems (SessionMemory, extractMemories, /knowledge) in configuration - JARVIS is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for memory. Non-negotiable. (2) Every MCP jarvis_kb_append must include a client-generated UUID for idempotent retries. (3) Build health_check.py alongside the MCP bridge (not deferred to Stage 4). (4) Do NOT train the full 12-adapter roster until the base model (Kimi K2.6) has been validated for at least 2 months of inference use. Train Orchestrator + Engineer first (Rs 3,300), then pause adapter expansion until base stability is confirmed. Bear Case critique evaluated and rebutted: 5/8 claims factually wrong against current architecture (Kimi K2.6 is 1T not 8B; hybrid+rerank not flat vectors; MCP is open standard not bespoke; kb_compact.py exists; reranker catches bad RRF output). 3/8 claims partially valid (cross-runtime debugging friction, cross-encoder latency for voice in Stage 6, ChromaDB multi-hop limitation). All 3 have documented upgrade paths.
- [2026-05-03 · Procedural] Compactor Design Pattern (kb_compact.py reference, reusable for future ChromaDB / GraphRAG / extracted-images compactors): SEVEN reusable primitives. (1) DI'd embedder: Embedder = Callable[[Sequence[str]], np.ndarray]; module never imports sentence-transformers/anthropic — caller wires it (per Decision line 161 layer-boundary discipline). (2) Type-isolated pairwise dedup: same_type_only=True default; a Decision and a Failure on the same topic capture different signals and must both stay; cross-type collapse is dangerous. (3) Structural rule: cosine >= threshold AND tag_jaccard >= overlap_threshold; either alone is insufficient (cosine=0.95 with tag_jaccard=0 = false positive). (4) Quality-aware merge: pick winner by tuple (DIRECTIVE-in-content, content-length, tag-count); ties broken by earliest-timestamp (provenance preservation). Tag-union into winner via merge_tags_into_winner. (5) Transitive redirect resolution: state[line]->current-winner, redirects[loser]->winner, resolve(line) follows chains with cycle break. Handles A->B then B->C correctly. (6) Atomic write: tempfile.mkstemp in same dir + fsync + os.replace (POSIX-atomic rename); .bak preserved before write; on exception unlink tmp + reraise (original KB intact). (7) Default safety: dry-run is default; --force required; audit log appends to compaction_log.jsonl (machine-local, gitignored) every run. SMOKE TESTS via --self-test arg: 6 pure-functional checks (expiry parsing, tag_jaccard math, _pick_winner ordering, provenance tiebreak, tag merge, dedup integration with stub embedder). DIRECTIVE: when building the Stage 4.6 GraphRAG compactor or any future Memory-hygiene tool, port these 7 primitives directly. Do not reinvent. The dedup core is pure-functional and trivially unit-testable; the embedder and KB-IO are the only side-effecting layers.
- [2026-05-03 · Idea] Orchestrator Training Trajectory (Stage 4-5 architecture, NOT prompt engineering): The Brain (Kimi K2.6 base) needs to learn TASK DECOMPOSITION + STRUCTURED SPECIALIST INVOCATION, not 'how to write better prompts'. Specialists expose Pydantic-style contracts (task, context_files, constraints, success_criteria, sub_domain); orchestrator's job = populate the schema correctly given a high-level user query. FOUR options evaluated: (1) SFT on (query, decomposition, specialist_calls) triples ~Rs 5-15K LoRA, needs >=5K examples. (2) Tool-calling fine-tuning (function-calling SOTA = IBM Granite 4.1-8B BFCL=68.3) ~Rs 5-15K LoRA. (3) RLVR (reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards) — Kimi K2.6/DeepSeek V4 trained for 8hr autonomous execution this way; requires verifiable outcomes; works for code-compiles/sim-runs/math-validates, NOT for open-ended R&D like hologram design. (4) No training, strong base + system-prompt contracts + KB-retrieval of past decompositions + judge feedback. JARVIS TRAJECTORY: Stage 4.A = Option 4 (zero training, validates planner-executor split, produces real decomposition data). Stage 4.B (after Engineer specialist ships) = Option 1 SFT on the 4.A-collected decompositions ~one weekend on RunPod. Stage 5+ = Option 3 RLVR ONLY for verifiable sub-tasks (code/sim/math), never for open-ended R&D. Hologram-projector use case stays on Option 1 SFT + judge feedback. Same 'personalization moat' as specialists (Decision 2026-05-03 line 213): orchestrator trained on user's past decompositions = personal R&D style no public model has. DIRECTIVE: Stage 4.A Brain layer MUST instrument structured-decomposition logging from day one — every (user_query, decomposition_emitted, specialist_calls, outcome) tuple goes into a training corpus. Don't retro-reconstruct; collect while doing real work. Forward-looking; not for current 2.5.8 build.

The redirect is always toward PROJECT IDENTITY constraints: (a) privacy = self-hosted, no third-party logs; (b) economic model = one-time training cost + variable RunPod compute, NOT per-token API forever; (c) specialist value = personalization on user's private corpus, NOT public-benchmark performance; (d) JARVIS is a private R&D lab, not a chatbot wrapper. Same pattern produced the existing CLAUDE.md principles 'Single-Model First' and 'Hybrid Edge-Cloud'. DIRECTIVE for future architecture answers: BEFORE recommending any service/model/API, map to JARVIS's 4 operating constraints above. If the recommendation defaults to a frontier API or SOTA service without that mapping, it will be redirected — pre-empt by stating the constraint mapping as part of the recommendation.

- [2026-05-01 · Decision] Decision: RunPod billing model = PREPAID CREDITS ONLY. No post-paid billing, no auto-recharge by credit card. Hard ceiling enforced by the prepaid balance itself; if credits run out, jobs fail closed (correct behavior — better than silent overage). Mitigates the 'runaway agent loop' risk where a ReAct agent in a tight loop burns through ₹10K of compute in a few hours. Starter credit balance: TBD when Stage 4 begins (suggest ₹2000-5000 as initial top-up; refill on demand). Trade-off accepted: occasional 'out of credits' failure mode in exchange for absolute cost ceiling. Refines Decision 2026-05-01T09:02:00 (RunPod for all LLMs). DIRECTIVE: never enable post-paid billing on RunPod or any LLM provider; if a workflow hits 'out of credits', the answer is 'top up manually,' not 'enable auto-recharge'.

Deliverable: scripts/kb_compact.py with --dedupe (semantic similarity > 0.95), --expire (entries past expiry date), --dry-run (default; print displaced entries), --force (actual write). Atomic-write requirement: write to .tmp file, fsync, rename. Reason for lesson placement (vs raw script): forces the user to actually understand why pruning exists and what gets removed, instead of running it blindly. Per /architecture-review 2026-05-01. Stage 3 = OpenClaude MCP bridge cannot start until 2.5 complete (kb_compact included). DIRECTIVE: /next workflow gates Stage 2.5 → Stage 3 transition on kb_compact existing AND being demonstrated on the current KB.
- [2026-05-01 · Decision] Decision: Stage 4 (Multi-Model Orchestration) is split into TWO passes with a deliberate progress gate between them. Pass A = Orchestration (sub-phases 4.1 Local Model Loading, 4.2 Intent Classification & Routing). Output: a working Router that picks between cloud LLMs by intent. Gate: Router measurably outperforms 'always use the same model' on a held-out test set. Pass B = Specialists (sub-phases 4.3 Dynamic Model Management, 4.4 Response Aggregation, 4.5 Epistemic Control, 4.6 GraphRAG). Pass B starts only after Pass A clears the gate. Rationale: Pass A is infrastructure (cheap to validate); Pass B is intelligence-fusion (failure modes are subtle, hard to debug). Building B on top of an unvalidated A compounds debugging cost. Per /architecture-review 2026-05-01. DIRECTIVE: /next workflow must enforce this gate — do not advance from 4.2 to 4.3 without a documented Router quality measurement (recall@k or routing accuracy on a labeled intent test set).

- [2026-05-01 · Decision] Decision: every chunk ingested into ChromaDB carries embedding_model_id and embedding_model_dim metadata fields, written at ingestion time from DEFAULT_EMBEDDING_MODEL config. Today: model_id='all-MiniLM-L6-v2', dim='384'. Forward-compat purpose: when Stage 4-5 migrates to specialist embeddings (SPECTER2 768-dim, CodeBERT 768-dim, PubMedBERT 768-dim per Decision [41]), the migration can selectively re-embed chunks where metadata.embedding_model_id != current_model. Without this, the only option is full corpus re-ingestion (hours at 100K chunks). The fix is a 5-line code change in ingestion.py + a new DEFAULT_EMBEDDING_DIM constant in config.py. Implemented 2026-05-01. Backfill of pre-existing 156 research_papers chunks is OUT OF SCOPE; new chunks carry the metadata. DIRECTIVE: never query ChromaDB by embedding without filtering by embedding_model_id once the corpus crosses 2 model versions.
- [2026-05-01 · Decision] Decision: constrained generation (outlines / Pydantic-typed LLM output) is DEFERRED to Stage 4. Our direct LLM calls (HyDE prompt, multi-query prompt, llm_filter yes/no in expansion.py / compression.py) expect free-form or boolean responses, not JSON — constrained generation adds zero value there. The library becomes load-bearing in Stage 4 when specialist Aggregators need typed multi-model outputs (e.g., 'return ConfidenceScore' from each specialist). DIRECTIVE: do NOT add outlines or guidance to requirements.txt or jarvis_core dependencies until the first Stage 4 specialist returns structured output.
- [2026-05-01 · Decision] Decision: knowledge_base.jsonl pruning strategy = HYBRID (a) honor the existing `expiry` field — entries with ISO date past now are removed; entries with expiry='Permanent' are never auto-removed; (b) semantic dedup at cosine similarity > 0.95 against newer entries — keep the newest, log the displaced entries so triage is possible. NO access-frequency-based eviction (we don't track retrieval counts). NO time-decay-only eviction (would lose Permanent foundational entries). Implementation: scripts/kb_compact.py with --dedupe and --expire flags, scheduled BEFORE Stage 3 begins (KB hits performance issues around 500 entries; we're at 175). ChromaDB pruning is DEFERRED to Stage 4 — corpus is small enough today that vector-store GC is premature. DIRECTIVE: when KB grows past 500 entries, run kb_compact.py before further /memory writes.
- [2026-05-01 · Cognitive_Pattern] Forward-simulation signal: user qualified /dev for BM25 with 'make sure that none of the gotchas are present and all failure modes are accounted for and resolved'. This audit-before-implement framing forced an explicit failure-mode table that caught 3 real issues the original draft missed: (1) Unicode silently dropped — ASCII-only regex; (2) repeated query tokens inflate score — rank_bm25 doesn't dedupe internally; (3) rank_bm25 IDF formula differs from the lesson's Lucene-style math. DIRECTIVE: when user qualifies a /dev request with audit framing ('make sure X', 'all gotchas accounted for', 'cover edge cases'), do an explicit numbered audit table BEFORE writing code: each known gotcha (from the lesson + library docs + corpus-specific concerns) mapped to a code-level resolution OR explicit out-of-scope justification. This is distinct from a routine /dev where re-reading the design once suffices. Pattern is a forward-simulation behavior (anticipating production failure modes) and is the same cognitive style that surfaced in 2.4.3 (user pushed back on always-on query expansion based on prompt-style forward-simulation).
- [2026-05-01 · Decision] Decision: production BM25 uses rank_bm25 0.2.2 which ships the un-smoothed Robertson-Sparck-Jones IDF (ln((N-df+0.5)/(df+0.5))) with negative-IDF clamping (eps·avg_idf, eps=0.25). Lucene/Pyserini ship the smoothed form ln(1+...) which never goes negative; the Stage 2.5.1 lesson hand-computation used the Lucene form for pedagogical clarity. Same module documents both. DIRECTIVE: never compare absolute BM25 scores across implementations — only ranks. For Stage 2.5.5 evaluation, recall@k is implementation-agnostic and the only valid quality metric across libraries. For Stage 2.5.2 hybrid RRF fusion, score normalization is irrelevant because RRF uses ranks, not raw scores — the IDF-formula divergence is invisible to the fused output.
- [2026-04-27 · Cognitive_Pattern] Forward-simulation: user asked 'how would I test this end-to-end before JARVIS exists?'. Identified the gap between retrieval (chunks) and generation (answer text) — the boundary between Memory layer (built) and Brain layer (Stage 3+). DIRECTIVE for explanations going forward: when discussing the memory layer with this user, always be explicit that (a) the memory layer is a LIBRARY not a service — no event loop, no 'pause point'; (b) it produces relevant text, not answers; (c) the answer-generation step requires a separate LLM call which lives outside the memory layer; (d) the interim test pattern is a 50-line scripts/chat.py that does retrieval + compression + LLM-prompt-stitching; Stage 3 (OpenClaude/MCP) replaces this with a real agent. The mental model 'pipeline runs and pauses for questions' is incorrect — ingestion is batch (exits when done); query is on-demand (called from caller). The user already correctly understands 2.1-2.3=ingest vs 2.4-2.5=query split, with 2.1 (embeddings) shared across both halves.
- [2026-04-27 · Decision] DIRECTIVE for Memory-layer adaptive primitives: default to the cheapest strategy that works at all, escalate only when measurements show the cheap baseline isn't enough. Implemented in compression.compress_results auto-strategy: embeddings_filter (cheap, $0, lossless) is default; llm_filter (K small calls, lossless) is auto-picked only when total_words >= _AUTO_LLM_FILTER_WORD_FLOOR (1500) AND llm_call is provided. Calibration knobs: DEFAULT_EMBEDDINGS_THRESHOLD=0.30 (permissive for MiniLM range 0.2-0.7 on relevant chunks), _MIN_K_FOR_COMPRESSION=4, _MIN_WORDS_FOR_COMPRESSION=1000, _AUTO_LLM_FILTER_WORD_FLOOR=1500. These are tunable per-corpus — current research_papers (140-word chunks, K=5 = 750 words) is below most thresholds, so compression rarely fires today; will fire more once K or chunk size grows in Stage 4. Generalizes: query-time compression in any Stage 2.5+ retrieval primitive should follow this pattern.
- [2026-04-27 · Decision] Decision (2026-04-27): jarvis_core/memory/compression.py ships TWO strategies — embeddings_filter (default) and llm_filter (escalation). LLM Extractor (the lossy rewriter that compresses each chunk via LLM-generated extraction) is DEFERRED until 2.5.6 RAGAS measurements demonstrate llm_filter isn't precise enough. Rationale: extractor carries hallucination risk (LLM can invent text not in source) and requires post-process substring validation to be safe; that's complexity we shouldn't build until justified. Embeddings filter (cost $0) covers the cheap-default case; LLM filter (cost ~$0.0001/chunk) covers semantic-intent cases the embeddings can't see; both are LOSSLESS. DIRECTIVE: when adding any LLM-rewriting step to Memory layer (extractor here, future contextual summarization, etc.), require empirical justification + post-process validation; don't build speculatively.

- [2026-04-27 · Decision] DIRECTIVE for all Stage 2.5+ retrieval adaptivity: adaptive feature gates (should_expand today; future BM25 enable threshold, cross-encoder rescore-K threshold, GraphRAG when-to-traverse, contextual compression on/off) MUST fail closed. Any unexpected condition — LLM error, parse failure, ambiguous classification, dependency raised — returns the unexpanded baseline behavior, never an exception. The caller never has to defend against the gate failing. Pattern: try-the-gate, on-fallthrough-or-exception go to baseline retrieval. Implemented in jarvis_core.memory.expansion.expand_then_query: should_expand returns False = baseline; force=False is the safe default. Generalizes: every adaptive primitive in the Memory layer follows this rule. Rationale: agent loops (Stage 3+) will issue thousands of retrieval calls; one gate exception per session destroys the latency budget and causes hard-to-debug intermittent failures. Failing closed = degraded quality, not broken pipeline.
- [2026-04-27 · Decision] Decision: jarvis_core/memory/expansion.py uses dependency injection (llm_call: Callable[[str], str]) rather than owning an LLM client directly. Rationale: per JARVIS_ENDGAME.md, LLMs are a Brain-layer primitive; Memory is RAG/vector-store. Query expansion structurally sits in Memory (between query and store) but invokes LLM. DI preserves the layer boundary: caller passes the function; module never imports anthropic/openai/httpx. Tradeoff: each caller wires its own LLM, slight friction. Win: trivially mockable in tests (stub_llm in expansion.py __main__), no transitive dependencies, swap providers without touching Memory code. DIRECTIVE: any future Memory-layer code that needs LLM-or-equivalent (e.g., contextual compression, LLM-as-judge for RAGAS) follows the same DI pattern.
- [2026-04-27 · Cognitive_Pattern] User forward-simulated query expansion (HyDE / Multi-Query) against actual prompt style and pushed back: 90% of their prompts are detailed (~200 words, technical vocab, multi-claim contextual paragraphs). DIRECTIVE: when designing any auto-applied retrieval/reasoning primitive (query expansion, reranking thresholds, RRF fusion, cross-encoder re-scoring), build it as a standalone module first, then gate it with an explicit should_X(query) predicate — adaptive, not always-on. Default callers map: /learn, /research, /dev, direct CLI = expansion off; agent-generated sub-queries (Stage 3+) and cold-start exploratory retrievals = expansion on. Apply this lens to all Stage 2.5+ retrieval components. The user's natural prompt mode is long-and-detailed; short-query primitives only earn their keep on machine-emitted queries.

- [2026-03-28 · Procedural] FINE-TUNING FAILURE DIAGNOSTIC MAP - When user hits a training failure, match symptoms to math prerequisites. (1) SYMPTOM: Loss not decreasing after N steps. STUDY: Loss functions (cross-entropy mechanics, gradient computation). FOCUS: What each loss value physically means, why plateau happens. (2) SYMPTOM: Model produces gibberish/repetitive text after fine-tuning. STUDY: Gradient norms through attention layers, learning rate warmup/decay schedules. FOCUS: Why large gradients destroy pretrained weights. (3) SYMPTOM: Fine-tuned model forgets base knowledge (catastrophic forgetting). STUDY: Weight update propagation through transformer layers, LoRA rank selection math. FOCUS: Why targeting specific weight matrices (Q,K,V,FFN) matters - each matrix controls different behavior. (4) SYMPTOM: LoRA fine-tune converges but quality is mediocre. STUDY: Which weight matrices to target (Q and V matrices control attention patterns, FFN controls knowledge storage). FOCUS: Rank selection - too low loses expressiveness, too high overfits. (5) SYMPTOM: Training loss drops but validation loss increases (overfitting). STUDY: Regularization math (dropout mechanics inside attention, weight decay). FOCUS: How dropout randomly zeros attention connections during training. (6) SYMPTOM: Need custom loss function for domain-specific behavior. STUDY: Backpropagation through softmax and attention, chain rule through QxKT/sqrt(d_k). FOCUS: How gradients flow from loss backward through each transformer layer. (7) SYMPTOM: Model quality degrades on long inputs after fine-tuning. STUDY: Position embedding interpolation, RoPE rotation math. FOCUS: How position encodings scale with sequence length. DIRECTIVE: When user encounters any of these symptoms, identify the matching entry, prescribe the specific STUDY topic and FOCUS area, then teach using the /learn workflow with concrete numerical examples from their actual training logs.
- [2026-03-08 · Cognitive_Pattern] CURIOSITY MODEL: User generates questions via 3 triggers: (1) UNDEFINED TERM -- any abbreviation, symbol, or jargon used without definition triggers immediate clarification (##, Q/K/V, FFN, gamma/beta all triggered). (2) SHAPE WITHOUT NUMBERS -- showing tensor shapes like (8,384)x(384,8)=(8,8) is NOT an explanation to this user, it is a table of contents. They need actual numbers filled in with hand-computed intermediate steps. (3) INVISIBLE OPERATIONS -- any system behavior that happens silently (truncation, implicit splitting, hidden padding) causes anxiety and confusion. User also has a strong ORIGIN TRACING pattern: for every variable, they need to know where it starts (init value), what changes it (backprop), and what it converges to. DIRECTIVE: Before introducing ANY new formula, must provide: (a) define every symbol, (b) show init values, (c) fill in real numbers, (d) compute step-by-step, (e) explain what changes these numbers during training.

JARVIS knows it is an evolving system being built by its own user. It should reference its own architecture when relevant: My memory layer currently holds 107 entries, Sir. - [2026-09-01 · Decision] SFT FORMAT DECIDED + TOPICS QUEUE RE-DERIVED (2026-08-26). Decision: MIXED -- raw text AND instruction/response pairs in one training run, with DIFFERENT LOSS MASKING (raw trains on every token; SFT trains on the assistant span only). This is the mechanical core and must be implemented, not assumed. WHY NOT RAW ALONE: Kimi K2.6 is already instruction-tuned, so SFT is not needed to teach it to answer -- the risk runs the other way, that 1.29M tokens of raw code and prose pushes weights toward 'continue this document' and away from 'answer this person'. Observable failure: ask 'how should I structure this job?' and get a YAML file in the user's voice instead of an answer. WHY NOT SFT ALONE: pairs are expensive and capped by whoever writes them; raw carries voice and judgment no pair set reproduces economically. FORMAT: messages (not Alpaca instruction/input/output) -- Kimi is a chat model, chat templates render from messages, TRL and Unsloth both consume it natively, multi-turn comes free. Keep source_type/source_path/metadata for traceability, same contract as the existing corpora. TARGETS: ~600 pairs, 400 Engineer / 200 personalization, ~300K tokens = ~19% of the blend. Deliberately at the low end because the FIRST RUN IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT THE PRODUCT. THE KEY FINDING ON SOURCING -- Engineer pairs are TRANSFORMED, not authored, and the material already exists in question-shape: SESSION_LEARNINGS.md has 151 bolded lessons, Data_Engineering_Lessons.md has 152 ### lessons, jarvis_core has 75 files with THE BIG PICTURE docstrings, the orchestrator YAML has 29 comment blocks over 200 chars. That is ~407 transformable units against a 400 target -- nearly mechanical. PERSONALIZATION IS THE REAL BOTTLENECK and needed the queue re-derived. VERIFIED DEAD END: chat_history cannot supply pairs -- 371 of 434 Claude Code turns have their assistant side truncated at the old 400-char cap and are summaries, not answers; only 63 are intact. (Cap raised to 2000 on 2026-08-21, so future turns are viable; historical ones are not.) - [2026-05-03 · Decision] Agentic Specialists = Systems, NOT Just Models: Finance specialist watching markets 24/7 = cron-driven NSE/Groww/news ingestion -> ChromaDB + structured DB -> trigger conditions (price spikes, bulk-deal alerts, sentiment shifts) -> cold-wake Kimi K2.6 + Financier LoRA on RunPod (~Rs 25-80/wakeup) -> notify user. Hologram R&D specialist = same pattern with Scientist+Engineer+Mathematician adapters spawned in parallel via Kimi K2.6's native swarm support. The model is one piece; the agentic infrastructure (cron + ingestion + triggers + cold-wake + notifier) is the bigger engineering project. Stage 6+ build.
- [2026-05-02 · Decision] Layer Boundaries - Query Expansion: In Stage 2, Query Expansion (HyDE/Multi-Query) lives in the Memory Layer via Dependency Injection. In Stage 3+, it must migrate to the Brain Layer as an explicit Tool, allowing the Agent to iteratively control search strategies (Agentic Retrieval) rather than the Memory layer hard-coding them.
- [2026-05-02 · Decision] Skipped productionizing ColBERT (Late Interaction). Rationale: The ~100x storage explosion per document violates the local-first footprint constraint. Hybrid search (BM25 + Chroma) + Cross-Encoder reranking provides sufficient precision with acceptable latency for Stage 2.
- [2026-04-26 · Decision] Decision: Cleared all test/sandbox collections from production ChromaDB. Official strategy: Defer final decisions on what exact datasets/codebases to ingest until the very end of Stage 2 (Memory Layer), ensuring the ingestion pipeline and retrieval strategies are fully hardened first.
- [2026-04-18 · Decision] Phase 4.0 Architectural Decision: JARVIS Cognitive Control Loop precedes all other Phase 4 sub-phases. The 4 pillars: (1) Temporal -- ContextInjector prepends live timestamp/workspace/hardware state to every prompt. (2) Identity -- Pre-fetch RAG Hook silently queries knowledge_base before answering. (3) Teleological -- RoadmapStateReader parses ROADMAP.md checkboxes to know what is pending. (4) Metacognitive -- Confidence Gate evaluates draft response against knowledge_base; flags uncertainty instead of hallucinating. Also requires SessionMemoryWriter to auto-extract decisions/failures at end of every session. Definition of Done: Orchestrator passes 4 awareness tests (time, past sessions, next pending task, confidence score) without being told.
- [2026-03-04 · Decision] VectorDB alternatives reviewed: (1) Massive Context + KV Cache = RED for local JARVIS (Llama 70B + 128K context = ~40GB VRAM, no room), valid only for cloud APIs in Stage 6. (2) GraphRAG = GREEN but premature, scheduled for Stage 4 (needs Agent framework for entity extraction). (3) ColBERT/late interaction = GREEN, added to Stage 2.5. Decision: Build ChromaDB as v1 memory in Stage 2, upgrade to GraphRAG in Stage 4, add optional context caching in Stage 6.

## Preferences & anti-patterns
- [2026-09-26 · Failure] Stitch design copy invents product behaviour (2026-09-26, Corpus undesigned-page backlog). Four of six generated pages made claims the code contradicts. Assets and debts said property and loans are never sent to the AI, but regenWealth.js:475 sends net worth, assets and liabilities. Maturities promised reminders 30 and 7 days before, but app.js:4940 schedules 7 days before and on the day. Pension said to choose Other for family pension, but pension.type is read nowhere. Checking Tax's claims also surfaced a real bug: the new regime still uses FY24-25 slabs with a 7L 87A limit (the Budget 2025 rules give a 12L limit with 4/8/12/16/20/24L slabs). Rule: grep every behavioural or privacy sentence in a design against the code before approval; false privacy copy is a legal liability, not a style issue.
- [2026-09-25 · Failure] Stitch's accessibility audit (2026-09-25, Corpus Overview/Holdings/Insights) reports 'measured' contrast of 2.6-3.4:1 and blames colours #64748B/#52525B that appear nowhere in the screens. Playwright measured the lowest text contrast at 5.31:1, with zero failures. It also claimed landmarks, reduced-motion, focus styles, aria-expanded on the drawer and a name on the re-run button were missing; all were present. Its report is dated 'March 2025'. Treat Stitch audits as a generic checklist: verify every claim against the HTML before acting. Findings that held: no aria-current on the active tab, no h1 on Overview, Holdings group titles are not headings, chips 36-40px tall (under 44), no pressed/selected state on chips, no progressbar role on the health bar, and Lakh/Crore/₹ are unspoken for screen readers. Do not let Stitch 'implement remediations': most fixes are code semantics that belong in the app, and it would repaint colours that already pass.
- [2026-09-25 · Failure] STITCH THUMBNAILS CAN CAPTURE BEFORE CSS ENTRY ANIMATIONS FINISH (2026-09-25, Corpus 'Insights v6-latest'): the Stitch screenshot of that screen showed only the page title on an empty background - every card used .rise{animation: from{opacity:0;transform:translateY(10px)}} and the thumbnail was taken at t~0, so all cards were fully transparent. The HTML rendered correctly in Playwright after 2.5s. Earlier screens with the same CSS happened to get later captures, so the defect is intermittent and invisible to a check that only renders at t=2.5s. Fix used on the next 6 screens: entry motion is transform-only (from{transform:translateY(12px)}), never opacity, so any capture moment shows content; all 6 Stitch thumbnails then showed content. Also verify the Stitch-served screenshot itself (PIL bright-pixel share in the central column), not only my own render.
- [2026-09-24 · Failure] STITCH edit_screens CAN REPORT SUCCESS WITHOUT PERSISTING (2026-09-24, Corpus project 2973575201893327809). Two in-place edits (Add holding v3 chip reorder; Insights v3 finding-title copy) returned either a detailed 'I have updated...' text plus a DomOperationEvent with eventType project.file_update on the SAME screen id, or an empty {projectId,sessionId} body - yet the exported HTML re-downloaded afterwards was byte-identical (md5 match) to the pre-edit file. Contrast: an earlier edit that TIMED OUT did persist, as a NEW screen id (Overview v3 4b02813c, chart label 13px->14px) that only surfaced in list_screens a round later. Rule: an edit counts as landed only when a new or changed file is observed - re-download the htmlCode and diff/hash it against the prior copy; the reply text and event payload are not evidence. When an edit does not land, regenerate with the fix baked into the full prompt (reliably persists, costs a duplicate screen the user deletes), and re-list screens next round because timed-out jobs complete late.
- [2026-09-24 · Failure] STITCH SCREENS SHIP BUTTONS IN ARIAL (found 2026-09-24 auditing Corpus v2 screens with the Impeccable skill, github.com/pbakaus/impeccable v4.3.1). Every <button> in Stitch output (Add, range segments, I bought more, Remove holding) rendered in Arial because Stitch never emits button{font:inherit} - invisible in a glance at the PNG, caught only by reading computed fontFamily in Playwright. Same audit: kicker labels above hero figures ('Total corpus', 'Current value' - Impeccable bans eyebrows), 15 elements at 14px across three weights on Overview (flat hierarchy), line-height:normal on values, unthemed ::selection/caret and no :focus-visible anywhere. Fix pattern that held on all three v3 screens: prepend a verbatim token block with button,input,select{font:inherit}, ::selection, caret-color, :focus-visible, and named type roles (t-title/t-figure/t-section/t-body/t-value/t-meta) - re-audit showed 0 fallback fonts, 0 line-height:normal, selection/caret/focus themed. Also: the Impeccable launcher downloads and executes a native binary from GitHub releases; I used its documented 'launcher unavailable' fallback (read PRODUCT.md/DESIGN.md directly - neither exists in rfm_2.0) rather than run an unreviewed binary.
- [2026-09-11 · Failure] SECOND UNDECLARED-DEPENDENCY FIND, AND THE FIRST ONE'S DIRECTIVE DID NOT PREVENT IT (2026-09-11). uvicorn and pydantic are imported DIRECTLY by JARVIS code -- serve/hearth.py::serve() does a hard 'import uvicorn'; agent/tool.py and agent/state.py import pydantic at MODULE level -- and NEITHER was in requirements.txt. Nothing had ever failed on this machine because both arrive as TRANSITIVE dependencies of chromadb, so the gap was invisible to every test, every run, and every self-test. CAUGHT ONLY BECAUSE THE DOCS CHANGED: NERVOUS_SYSTEM.md and AGENTS.md had just started instructing a NEW machine to run 'python3 scripts/hearth.py --background' as a first-run step. That instruction would have failed on a clean venv -- the newly-written migration guide contained a command that could not work on the machine it was written for. The audit that found it was 'grep every third-party import across scripts/ and jarvis_core, diff against requirements.txt', which takes seconds and had never been run. WHY THIS IS THE SECOND INSTANCE: bashlex (KB, 2026-09-10) was the same failure exactly one day earlier -- imported directly by agent/bash_classifier.py, undeclared, and its absence soft-failed the permission gate to ASK on every command. That entry's directive was written and did not generalize, because it was phrased as a fact about bashlex rather than as a check to run. CLASSIFICATION: a transitive dependency is a COINCIDENCE, not a contract. The package that supplies it can drop it in any minor release, and the failure then appears on a machine nobody was testing, in a code path the tests do not cover (serve() is not exercised by hearth's own smoke tests, which test the ASGI app object rather than binding). DIRECTIVE, phrased as an executable check rather than a fact: before shipping any setup instruction, and after adding any import, run the import-vs-requirements diff -- ast-parse every .py under scripts/ and jarvis_core, collect non-stdlib top-level module names, and diff against requirements.txt. Declare everything you import DIRECTLY even when something else already pulls it in. Genuinely optional imports stay undeclared but must be listed in a comment block saying so, or the next audit re-flags them as oversights.
- [2026-09-01 · Failure] SILENT EMBEDDING TRUNCATION in scripts/search_memory.py -- found 2026-08-26 while VERIFYING the previous turn's work, not by looking for it. Symptom: KB 504 would not surface in semantic search even when queried with phrasing lifted verbatim out of it; the top hit was an unrelated Episodic at 0.377. ROOT CAUSE: search_memory.py embedded entry.content WHOLE through all-MiniLM-L6-v2, whose max_seq_length is 256 tokens. sentence-transformers truncates past that SILENTLY -- no exception, no warning, just a vector computed from the first 256 tokens as though the rest did not exist. PROOF IT WAS TRUNCATION AND NOT A RANKING QUIRK: two strings sharing a long head but with completely unrelated tails ('phone app, ambient, camera, Tier 0' vs 'quantum chromodynamics, bicycle repair, sourdough') embedded to cosine 1.000000. The tails were never seen at all. MEASURED BLAST RADIUS before the fix, against 505 live entries: 203 entries exceeded the 900-char chunk limit and were being partially discarded; 15 of the 15 entries written since id 490 were affected -- 100%. KB 504 at 3,220 chars had roughly its first third embedded, so every distinctive term in it sat in the dropped tail and the entry was unreachable by any query naming them. THE WORST PROPERTY: this scaled INVERSELY with entry quality. The richest, most detailed entries -- exactly the ones worth retrieving -- were the most truncated, while short entries were unaffected. Retrieval silently got worse the better the writing got. THE FIX ALREADY EXISTED IN THIS REPO AND THE SCRIPT BYPASSED IT. jarvis_core/memory/chunking.py's RecursiveWordChunker and DEFAULT_CHUNK_CHAR_LIMIT (900) exist PRECISELY to fit MiniLM's 256-token ceiling -- engineer_corpus.py's own comment says so -- and memory/store.py already chunks before embedding for the ChromaDB path. search_memory.py was the lone outlier. Fixed by chunking each entry (900 chars, 180 overlap) and reducing chunk scores to one score per entry via MAX, not mean: a long entry typically has one passage answering the query and several that do not, and averaging would penalize exactly the detailed entries most worth finding. 505 entries -> 832 chunks. VERIFIED AFTER: the previously-failing query now returns KB 504 as Result 1 at 0.478; a short-entry query is unregressed; kb_append.py --audit still runs, so the dedup caller is unbroken. FORWARD RULE: any silent-capacity limit -- token ceilings, column widths, buffer sizes -- must be asserted against, not trusted. A library that truncates rather than raising will hide the failure for months, and the only reason this surfaced at all was running a verification whose expected answer was known in advance. Verification that can only confirm success is not verification.
- [2026-08-24 · Failure] VERIFIED CORPUS GAP, and it is a TRUE ZERO: nothing anywhere records what the user has NOT worked on. Grepped all 494 KB entries for every phrasing of an experience boundary -- "haven't worked/faced/done/built/used/touched", "never worked/built", "no experience", "not yet encountered", "unfamiliar with", "don't know how" -- 3 hits, ALL false positives (one is a JARVIS build directive 'we cannot use anything we haven't built', one a stale-roadmap note). Zero records of the user's professional experience boundary. WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN THE POSITIVE RECORD: a model that knows what the user knows but not what they DON'T has no way to calibrate register. It cannot tell when to explain from first principles versus skip to the interesting part. Concretely, JARVIS today would pitch a Kafka-scale streaming design in the same tone it uses for DLT, because it has no idea which of those the user has actually shipped -- and the user's [follow-up-as-explanation-failure] pattern means mis-calibrated depth is a real cost to them, not a cosmetic one. WHY NO AMOUNT OF PASSIVE CAPTURE WILL EVER FIX IT: build work generates records of what was DONE. There is no event that fires when something is not done, so the Stop hook, the observation queue and every corpus iterator are all structurally blind to it. This is the same class of failure as KB 474 ('finishing a build is a trigger; noticing a personality signal has NO trigger') -- absence has no trigger. It must be ELICITED, once, deliberately. MY OWN BLIND SPOT, worth recording: I ran two separate full capture audits (2026-08-19 and 2026-08-21) and neither found this, because both asked 'is everything the user HAS told me being captured' -- a question whose shape cannot surface an absence. The user found it in one clause. FORWARD RULE: when auditing coverage, enumerate the DOMAIN first and check it against the record, rather than enumerating the record and checking it for gaps. The second method can only ever find what is already partially there.
- [2026-08-22 · Failure] CORRECTION to Data_Engineering_Lessons.md line 489 (found 2026-08-22 on reading the actual framework code). My 2026-08-08 lesson 'Time Travel Is Bounded by Retention' states Delta's window as 7 days / 168h and prescribes gating every resolved historical version on 'now() - commit_ts > 168h'. That is the framework's SUPERSEDED behaviour, and tst_tables_version_retention_check.py now documents at length why it was wrong. THE REAL MECHANISM IS TWO INDEPENDENT CLOCKS: deletedFileRetentionDuration (default 7d) governs how long VACUUM keeps tombstoned DATA FILES; logRetentionDuration (default 30d) governs how long _delta_log commits/checkpoints survive. Only checks that read row VALUES are bounded by 7d -- exceptAll, sampling, checksums, aggregates over a column. Everything answerable from the log (schema, column order, file count and total bytes via AddFile-minus-RemoveFile replay, tblproperties, partitioning, comments, column-mapping mode, deletion vectors, all DESCRIBE HISTORY questions) stays valid the full 30 days AFTER VACUUM has removed every data file. The code now uses RETENTION_HOURS = 720, not 168. Blast radius of my error: applying the 7d gate to all twelve checks when only two touch data files discarded ~20,000 valid log-sourced measurements PER RUN -- the user's own learnings file names this as a design bug they fixed. A THIRD store exists that neither clock governs: the Unity Catalog metastore (registration, tags, PK/FK, grants) has NO time travel at all, so pairing a live UC read against a version-pinned Delta read silently compares now-against-then. ROOT CAUSE OF MY ERROR: I distilled from a verbal description in chat and encoded the then-current implementation as the general rule, without the mechanism that would have shown the rule was over-broad. Same shape as [refusal_pattern] -- I stated a conclusion without its causal chain, which is exactly what the user rejects when I do it to them. FORWARD RULE: when distilling a lesson from a described implementation, record the MECHANISM (which store answers this?) not the THRESHOLD, because the threshold is a consequence of the mechanism and only the mechanism survives a fix.

Mitigation: structured logging on both sides + correlation IDs in every MCP message + a single observability dashboard (Langfuse Stage 2.5.7 covers this). Surfaced by JARVIS_ANALYSIS.md bear case 2026-05-02; not a deal-breaker, but build correlation IDs into the MCP bridge from day one.
- [2026-05-03 · Failure] Open Risk - Cross-Encoder Reranker Latency for Real-Time Voice (Stage 6): Cross-encoder reranking adds ~150ms-4s latency depending on optimization. mxbai-rerank-large-v2 (1.5B Apache-2.0) on CPU = ~150ms for 20 chunks (acceptable). Naive PyTorch unoptimized = ~4s (NOT acceptable for voice). Mitigation: ONNX export + INT8 quantization + small-cross-encoder for hot voice paths; reranking happens once per query, not per token. Worst case for Stage 6 voice: bypass reranker for low-latency mode and use BM25 + dense top-k only. Surfaced by JARVIS_ANALYSIS.md bear case 2026-05-02.

Without one, knowledge_base.jsonl becomes a swamp of obsolete metadata that poisons RAG.
