- [2026-05-02 · Episodic] User pushed back on a BLOCKED verdict. The system misinterpreted a clarifying question about the next implementation step (/dev) as an attempt to bypass a concept check. Directives: Do not penalize users for confirming the architectural plan before executing.
- [2026-04-27 · Failure] Streaming Delta tables retain columns from ALL writers forever. When a DLT streaming table is written by multiple @dp.append_flow flows (e.g., historical Parquet + ongoing CloudFiles), Delta unions their schemas. Once any writer produces a column, it is permanent in the table — subsequent writes that don't produce it land NULL. Removing the column requires drop+recreate because DLT-managed streaming tables don't enable column mapping mode. Lesson: NORMALIZE column schemas across all writers BEFORE they ever run, or accept permanent NULL-padding. Common manifestation: historical loader produces a deprecated column, you remove it from the incremental loader, but it stays in the table forever as a NULL graveyard.
- [2026-04-27 · Failure] create_auto_cdc_flow with stored_as_scd_type=1 implements per-key overwrite ONLY: key in source not in target = INSERT; key in both = UPDATE/overwrite; key in target NOT in source = UNTOUCHED (row persists forever). There is NO equivalent to MERGE WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE DELETE. SCD1 specifies history behavior for matched keys; it says nothing about orphan target rows. When this bites: (a) authoritative snapshot sources where key disappearance should propagate as delete; (b) PK-column restatement where business-column change shifts the key hash, inserting NEW row while old orphans. Options for delete-if-missing: switch to snapshot_cdc, OR union with anti-join + apply_as_deletes view, OR accept drift and monitor.
- [2026-04-27 · Failure] SUM/AVG/MAX over zero rows returns NULL, not 0. Downstream comparisons like result = 0 evaluate to NULL (falsy in WHERE) — tests 'fail' silently because NULL is neither equal nor not-equal to 0. Manifests as: a data-quality test that should catch a problem reports passing when the violating row count is zero (because the violating SELECT returned NULL not 0). Fix: always wrap aggregations with COALESCE(SUM(...), 0) when the WHERE clause could legitimately match zero rows. Universal across SQL engines, not Spark-specific.
- [2026-04-27 · Failure] Cross-job pipeline overlap fails immediately, not queued. max_concurrent_runs:1 only serializes runs of THE SAME job. Two different jobs triggering the same DLT pipeline at overlapping times = the second update gets rejected in ~1s with 'pipeline busy'. There is no cross-job pipeline queue at the platform level. No DAB-native clean solution. Mitigation menu (pick at least two): (a) staggered schedules, but manual triggers still break it; (b) task-level retries with 2-min backoff, but codegen regeneration wipes them; (c) master-job orchestrator using run_job_task to serialize; (d) consolidate to one owning job. Plan retries + staggered schedules from day one when a pipeline is shared across jobs.
- [2026-04-27 · Failure] Silent CSV/path quirks (defend BEFORE column-count validation): (1) Trailing commas on every row (Synapse Link, many ERP exports) -> Spark reads an extra empty _c<n> column. Defense: if len(df.columns) == len(expected_columns) + 1: df = df.drop(df.columns[-1]). (2) Leading/trailing spaces in folder/file names from upstream systems -> entity-lookup code that matches on name silently fails (entity not found). Defense: .strip() in lookup code; never trust upstream names to be clean. (3) schemaEvolutionMode: addNewColumns ONLY adds — never removes, renames, or changes types. Source removes a column -> Autoloader doesn't catch it -> column starts landing NULL silently. Schema-drift test should flag unexpected NULL patterns in previously-populated columns.
- [2026-04-21 · Failure] ANTI-PATTERN: Deleted .bin HNSW index files vs corrupted .bin files have different failure modes. Deleted = hard crash on query (traceable, loud). Corrupted = garbage results returned silently (dangerous, invisible). Both are defended against by full-directory .tar.gz backup (not just chroma.sqlite3). PRAGMA integrity_check covers SQLite only, not HNSW binary segments.
- [2026-04-17 · Failure] MOST DANGEROUS BUG IN PRODUCTION: Silent NULL from .cast('TIMESTAMP') on non-ISO dates. When US date format (M/d/yyyy h:mm:ss a) is cast with .cast('TIMESTAMP'), Spark returns NULL silently. No error, no warning. Pipeline succeeded, data looked complete, but ALL timestamp columns were NULL. Discovered only by manual inspection. FIX: Use to_timestamp(col, 'M/d/yyyy h:mm:ss a') with explicit format pattern. SELECT * EXCEPT(SinkCreatedOn, SinkModifiedOn), to_timestamp(SinkCreatedOn, 'M/d/yyyy h:mm:ss a') AS SinkCreatedOn. RULE: .cast() is safe for ISO 8601 dates but silently destroys non-ISO dates. ALWAYS verify type-cast columns have non-NULL values after first run.
- [2026-04-17 · Failure] DELTA TYPE WIDENING TRAP: DECIMAL and DOUBLE are incompatible type families in Delta Lake. delta.enableTypeWidening supports: INT->LONG, FLOAT->DOUBLE, DECIMAL(p1,s1)->DECIMAL(p2,s2) where p2>=p1. Does NOT support: DECIMAL->DOUBLE (precision loss) or DOUBLE->DECIMAL (quantization). Scenario: historical load writes Amount as DECIMAL(18,2) via schema transform. Incremental API source returns DOUBLE. Both append to same streaming table. Result: runtime failure. FIX: Align types at source -- add explicit CAST(Amount AS DECIMAL(18,2)) in incremental pipeline before write. RULE: Always cast to match the creator's type when multiple writers target the same table.
- [2026-04-17 · Failure] Column name casing creates silent data corruption in Spark. Different sources producing Line_Of_Business vs Line_of_Business become TWO separate columns in a UNION. Spark SQL is case-sensitive for column names in certain contexts. Fix: Establish a canonical column name registry. Enforce at the earliest ingestion point. Schema metadata queries are also case-sensitive: information_schema.columns stores table names in lowercase. Querying with original casing returns zero rows. Always LOWER() on both sides. Real incident: LRC project had Cost_Centre (API source) vs CostCentre (BYOD source) causing UNRESOLVED_COLUMN. Fix: standardize to CostCentre across ALL layers at the earliest point.
- [2026-04-17 · Failure] DLT REFERENCE_DLT_DATASET_OUTSIDE_QUERY_DEFINITION error: Referencing a pipeline dataset outside a dataset query definition (@dlt.table annotation) is not supported. WHEN it fails: if you unify STG and BRZ into one pipeline, spark.sql inside a Python callback (snapshot source_function) hits this error because the STG table is now an in-pipeline dataset. Cross-pipeline reads (STG in one pipeline, BRZ in another) are external UC table reads -- spark.sql handles these normally. Same-Pipeline STG+BRZ compatibility matrix: Regular CDC (mode: cdc, apply_changes) with delta source -> YES works. Snapshot CDC with source_table -> YES works. Snapshot CDC with source_function (Python callback with spark.sql) -> NO, fails. Keep split across 2 pipelines. Decision rule: if BRZ template injects a Python function as source, STG and BRZ MUST live in separate pipelines with depends_on.
- [2026-04-17 · Failure] Gen1 BRZ SCD2 Migration Problem -- Double SCD2 Breaks Active Records. Scenario: Migrating from Gen1 BRZ (already SCD2) to Gen2 BRZ (also SCD2). When Gen1 BRZ Parquet is exported as-is and loaded through Gen2 STG -> BRZ CDC, 100% of active rows end up with WRONG business values despite row counts matching perfectly. Why: Gen1 BRZ has multiple versions per PK with edp_is_current/edp_effective_dttm. Gen2 CDC picks ONE winner per PK based on sequence_by. But edp_effective_dttm is IDENTICAL for all versions. edp_record_insert_dttm on the EXPIRED version is often the LATEST -> CDC picks the wrong (expired) row. Evidence: ITEM_TRANS -- 628/628 ACCOUNT mismatches (100%), 623/628 MONETARY_AMOUNT sign flips. Fix Option 1: Filter historical load to WHERE edp_is_current = True -- 1 row per PK, preserves correct data, loses SCD2 history. LESSON: Running CDC on data that is already SCD2 creates double-SCD2 which sequence_by alone cannot resolve.
- [2026-04-17 · Failure] PeopleSoft Snapshot Function TIMESTAMP Cast Bug (resolved 2026-04). Original code used bare string comparison in Spark SQL: WHERE _source_file_modification_time > '{latest_snapshot_version}'. Fixed to: WHERE _source_file_modification_time > TIMESTAMP '{latest_snapshot_version}'. Without explicit TIMESTAMP cast, Spark's string-to-timestamp comparison is unreliable depending on the string representation of the Python object. Both comparison lines needed the fix. LESSON: ALWAYS use TIMESTAMP 'literal' when injecting a Python timestamp value into a Spark SQL string. Never rely on implicit string->timestamp comparison in Spark SQL. This applies to any Python f-string interpolation into Spark SQL temporal comparisons.
- [2026-04-05 · Failure] ANTI-PATTERN: OpenRouter free models block agentic tool-use in two failure modes. (1) 404 No endpoints found that support tool use: Model endpoint (e.g., google/gemma-*:free, minimax/minimax-m2.5:free) actively disables function/tool calling for policy reasons. OpenClaude requires tool-use for every request -- 100% fatal. (2) 429 Provider rate-limited upstream: Model is routed through a third-party GPU provider (Venice.ai, AkashML) that has its own quota independent of OpenRouter credits. Paying OpenRouter $10 does NOT buy more Venice/AkashML capacity. FIX: Only use models routing through first-party APIs. Indicator: error metadata field provider_name shows Venice or AkashML -> switch model immediately to deepseek/deepseek-v3.2 or gemini-2.0-flash.
- [2026-04-04 · Failure] ANTI-PATTERN: Windows Bun/Node TTY stdin detachment on cross-directory spawns. Root cause: when bun runs a child_process spawning an interactive Node.js CLI across a directory boundary, Windows fails to attach the keyboard stdin pipe. The CLI boots, renders banner, receives EOF on stdin, and silently exits with code 1. FIX: ALWAYS launch from the directory physically containing dist/cli.mjs. The jarvis.ps1 script enforces this with an explicit cd. Verified: bun run dev:codex works because package.json scripts resolve relative to their own directory. shell:false + absolute-path combos also fail on Windows because cmd.exe is required to resolve paths with spaces.
- [2026-03-01 · Failure] Common async confusions: (1) async with is NOT the event loop -- it is a resource lifecycle manager (open/close). asyncio.run() creates the event loop. (2) async def alone = coroutine (one result). async def + yield = async generator (streams). (3) async for is READ-ONLY consumption -- does NOT use send(). send() is the Phase 2 sync generator protocol. (4) gather() launches concurrent tasks -- it does not embed or store, those happen downstream.

User tests whether a stated limitation is FUNDAMENTAL or merely INCIDENTAL by proposing the one mechanism that would refute its stated justification, rather than accepting the rule at face value. Incident (2026-05-27, DE digest): digest claimed 'snapshot CDC (create_auto_cdc_from_snapshot_flow) is incompatible with continuous mode BECAUSE .collect()/spark.sql MIN-MAX cannot run in a streaming loop.' User countered: 'what if we wrap those batch ops in foreachBatch inside the source function?' -- foreachBatch is exactly the mechanism that DOES run batch ops in a streaming loop, so the question surgically falsifies the digest's stated reason. The probe was correct: the real constraint is not 'batch ops in a loop' but (a) the source function is a DLT-owned pull-based callback returning discrete (DataFrame, version) tuples with no streaming write to attach a sink to, and (b) snapshot diffing is intrinsically discrete/bounded. Teaching implication: when documenting a constraint for this user, state the FUNDAMENTAL why (API contract / semantics), never a convenient-but-incidental why -- he will find and break the weak justification. Distinct from but related to forward_simulated_architecture (stress-testing designs against future production state); this pattern stress-tests the *justification of an existing rule*.
